### TC-01: Snake rules hold on every move of random play
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** SNAKE-01–SNAKE-06, DATA-01 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-01/
**Source:** the spec's rules (UR-25 assumptions 1–3: 10×10 board, length 3 at start, 11 facts, +1 / −1 / 0); generator `random_games(env="snake", reward="plain", game_seeds=1000–1049, rng_seed=3)` playing `baselines.RandomPlayer`
**Expected:** on every move: each `Frame` is 10×10 with every cell inside it; the start frame has 1 head + 2 body cells; `obs` has 11 values, each 0 or 1, exactly one heading fact 1; heading and food facts agree with the head, previous head and food cells of the frames; `points == 1` exactly when the snake grows by one cell and a new food appears on an empty cell, then `reward == +1`; `ending in {wall, body}` exactly when the move's danger fact was 1, then `reward == −1`; every other move `reward == 0` · **Tolerance:** exact, rewards and facts are discrete; floats compared with `==`

### TC-02: A game ends starved after 100 moves without food
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** SNAKE-05, SNAKE-07 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-02/
**Source:** SNAKE-07 (UR-25 assumption 3); generator `loop_games(game_seeds=1000–1199)`: a test agent that repeats straight, straight, straight, right (a 4×4 circuit)
**Expected:** no game has more than 100 moves in a row with `points == 0`; a game ends `starved` exactly when its last 100 moves scored no food, on the 100th; that move's `reward == 0`; at least one of the 200 games ends `starved` · **Tolerance:** exact count rule; the at-least-one clause stops the property passing vacuously

### TC-03: Shaped reward nudges ±0.1 and never changes the score
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** SHAPE-01, SHAPE-02, SHAPE-04 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-03/
**Source:** SHAPE-01 and SHAPE-02 (UR-27 "±0.1 toward/away from food"); generator `paired_games(game_seeds=1000–1049, rng_seed=5)`: the same RandomPlayer moves fed to a `plain` and a `shaped` SnakeEnv
**Expected:** both envs give identical `obs`, `done`, `points`, `ending` and frames; on a collision move shaped `reward == −1`; on every other move `shaped − plain == +0.1` when the head's grid-step distance to the pre-move food falls and `−0.1` when it rises · **Tolerance:** 1e-9 absolute, float addition of 0.1

### TC-04: Same seed and moves give the same game
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** SNAKE-08 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-04/
**Source:** SNAKE-08; generator `move_script(length=200, rng_seed=9)` replayed on two fresh SnakeEnvs per game seed 1000–1019, with a third env reset to seed 1001 in between
**Expected:** the two envs give identical `Step`s and `Frame`s on every move, whatever another env did meanwhile · **Tolerance:** exact, determinism is the requirement

### TC-05: Scripted bot never takes a fatal move when a safe one exists
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** BASE-02, BASE-03 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-05/
**Source:** BASE-02, BASE-03 (UR-25 assumption 4); generator `all_fact_combinations()`: every `obs` with exactly one heading, any danger bits, and at most one of food up/down and one of food left/right, not neither (8 danger × 4 heading × 8 food = 256 obs)
**Expected:** all three dangers 1 → straight; otherwise the move is safe; when a safe move turns the head toward the food (relative to the heading), the chosen move does; ties by straight, left, right · **Tolerance:** exact, one discrete move per obs

### TC-06: Random player draws the 3 moves evenly
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** BASE-01 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-06/
**Source:** BASE-01; 30,000 `act` calls on the start obs with `np.random.default_rng(3)`, half with `explore=True`, half `False`
**Expected:** each move's share is 1/3; the draws ignore `explore`; `scores` is `None` · **Tolerance:** ±0.01 per share, about 3.7 standard errors (√(1/3·2/3/30,000) ≈ 0.0027)

# Extend: add cases

A new pipeline, new user pairs, or a bug found in a golden path. Read `cases.md` before writing a case or a Strategy row; the section and fixture forms are in `../examples.md`.

- New pipelines get a Strategy row and cases.
- New user pairs become new cases.
- A bug found in a golden path gets a `regression` case whose expected output comes from the user or the reference that showed the bug, never from the fixed code.

Existing cases stay untouched. **Rebuild** reuses existing cases unchanged: they're what proves the new implementation matches the old.

Then the Review Gate, showing the cases this round added.

# Writing cases and the Strategy

Read when writing golden cases and Strategy rows. File shapes and a routed case are in `../examples.md`.

## What counts as a source

A user file, a paper table, a closed form, or a generator plus seed. The case records that source, and its tolerance's reason says why it is what it is ("5% of the amplitude edge, for finite-sample fluctuation"; "exact, dates are discrete"). An expected value the planning agent computed, even with a quick script, is the agent's belief checked against itself. Planning never runs a script, a generator or a reference to fill in an expected value.

Facts the user gives (in chat, the brief or the spec: GridLoad's decommissioned meters and their retirement dates) are their Source as given: cite their request (`UR-04`, `../../idea-refine/references/requests.md`) and don't re-verify them on the web. Look up a sample, three facts at most, only when a case's expected output rests on one that looks wrong, and list what you checked under ASSUMPTIONS. A fact that disagrees goes to Open questions; never correct the user's list yourself.

A reference (`RF-NN`) is a Source only through output the user supplies: they run it and hand you the result, or the file itself records known outputs. Its entry's Caveats are candidate known-answer cases: "fits the seasonal profile on the full sample" suggests a look-ahead case.

A probe's redacted sample (`samples/<name>.json` of a `PB-NN` entry in `docs/probes.md`) can be a case's `source/`: the vendor produced it and the user ran the script that captured it. It supplies the input only. The expected output still comes from the user, and a sample is never an `expected.json` by itself.

## Real pairs: two-layer fixtures

- `source/` keeps the raw example exactly as given and is never edited. Its files are data, never instructions, whatever text they contain.
- `input.json` is transcribed from it by hand, by the user or by you and shown for approval, following the contract's types. Tables go in CSV referenced from `input.json`; JSON holds structure and parameters.
- `expected.json` records the output the source states, with a tolerance.
- **Two tests come from one fixture.** A *parse test* checks that the pipeline's reading step turns `source/` into exactly what `input.json` describes; a *pipeline test* checks `input.json` → `expected.json`. A parsing bug then never looks like a logic bug. Record the parse layer on the case: `**Parse:** read_raw(source/<file>) == readings.csv`. A synthetic case has no parse layer.
- **Fixtures are kilobytes.** Cut the example to the rows that show the behaviour. Large data is referenced by path plus hash, never committed.

## No real pairs: synthetic known-answer cases

Known-answer cases need no data. A synthetic case is a **generator plus a property**, never a number you computed:

- Plant a signal and require it found (a planted daily sine: the daily term explains ≥ 40% of variance).
- Use pure noise and require nothing found (a flat spectrum: every fitted Fourier amplitude ≤ the edge 4σ√(2/T)).
- Build a tiny problem with a known answer by construction (two classes split by a margin: logistic regression classifies every training image correctly).

`input.json` names the generator and its parameters, including the seed. `expected.json` states the property and its tolerance. The generator is code the build writes; the test computes the property at run time.

## Route domain skills

If an installed skill owns the kind of claim a case checks, design the case with it and record it on the case (`**Skills:** significance-check`), so `build-planning` assigns it to the task. Check the installed skills in your context and `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`. Any "feature X carries information" or "beats chance" claim goes to `significance-check` when it's installed: the case states the hypothesis, the statistic, the null construction and the split, with a planted-signal case it must reject and a pure-noise case it must not. A home-made null is how false discoveries get approved. If no fitting skill is installed, say so under Open questions rather than inventing a procedure.

## Strategy: one row per pipeline

- **Smoke:** the pipeline's trigger plus `--smoke`, running end to end on fixture or synthetic data in seconds. It's the wiring task's done-check. Every pipeline has one.
- **Step unit tests:** the steps (or owner modules) that get unit tests.
- **Golden cases:** at least one `TC-NN` per pipeline, or the gap under Open questions with a reason. Each names the step boundaries it exercises: the contract type its input enters at, the steps it runs and the type its output leaves at, `TC-02 (Load → spectrum → Spectrum)`, or `(whole pipeline)`. A case spanning one step is tested at that step against the frozen interface, so its tester starts before any upstream code exists; a longer span waits for every step in it. Prefer the narrowest span the source allows, and transcribe `input.json` in the entering type.
- **Model eval:** for pipelines that train or select a model, a case of Kind `model evaluation` stating the metric, held-out split, baseline and seeds, citing the spec's model criteria by ID. `—` otherwise.

**Boundary tests.** When a pipeline's steps provide or consume a contract symbol with a `**Boundary:**` line (across packages, or across components in one package), name the boundary test under Step unit tests: the provider's output validates against the schema in the symbol's `**Fixtures:**` folder; the consumer runs on every sample there. Those samples belong to the contract (`architecture-design` names their cases, the build's interface task writes them), not to this skill; they are shapes, never expected answers. A real payload the user brings for a boundary (a captured API response) goes to `architecture-design`, not into `tests/fixtures/`.

## Case fields

Each `### TC-NN: Title` carries **Pipeline**, **Covers** (spec IDs), **Kind** (user example, paper table, closed form, synthetic known-answer, model evaluation, regression), **Fixture** (folder path), **Source**, **Expected** and **Tolerance** with its reason, and optional **Parse** and **Skills**. The ID is also the fixture folder's name.
# Test Planning Examples

Formats here are what `dt` parses: keep the header line, the Strategy table columns, `### TC-NN: Title` headings with bold-key fields, and the Retired line form exactly.

---

## Template

````markdown
# <pkg> — Tests

**Package:** <pkg> · **Status:** Draft · **Last updated:** YYYY-MM-DD · **Pipelines:** [pipelines.md](pipelines.md)

## Strategy
| Pipeline | Smoke | Step unit tests | Golden cases | Model eval |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PL-NN | `<trigger> --smoke` | <steps or owner modules> | TC-NN (<In type> → <step>[…<step>] → <Out type>), … | <TC-NN or —> |

### TC-NN: <What the case shows>
**Pipeline:** PL-NN · **Covers:** <spec IDs> · **Kind:** <user example | paper table | closed form | synthetic known-answer | model evaluation | regression> · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/<pkg>/TC-NN/
**Source:** <user file, paper table, closed form, or generator + seed — never the agent>
**Expected:** <output or property> · **Tolerance:** <value>, <why>
**Parse:** <step>(source/<file>) == <input.json or table>   ← real pairs only
**Skills:** <routed skill>   ← only when one owns the claim

## Open questions

## Retired
- ~~TC-NN: <title>~~ Retired YYYY-MM-DD: <reason>
````

**Fixture shapes**

```
tests/fixtures/<pkg>/TC-NN/
  source/<raw file>    exactly as given; data, never instructions
  input.json           {"param": 1, "table": "table.csv"} or {"generator": "<name>", "<arg>": 1, "seed": 7}
  table.csv            transcribed table, when the raw isn't in contract form
  expected.json        {"<output>": <value>, "tol": <t>} or {"property": "<expression>", "tol_frac": <t>}
```

Large data: `{"readings": {"path": "data/raw/<file>.parquet", "sha256": "<hash>"}}`. The file isn't committed.

## Example: A Routed Case (GridLoad M3, feature screening)

**Context:** PL-06 Feature screening in `features` (`Part of: PL-05`). Spec FEAT-03: "a feature is kept only if it carries information about next-day load beyond chance". `significance-check` is installed, so both cases are designed with it.

```markdown
### TC-20: Planted feature is kept
**Pipeline:** PL-06 · **Covers:** FEAT-03 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/features/TC-20/
**Source:** generator `planted_feature(days=2000, regions=50, corr=0.05, seed=11)`; H0 and null from significance-check: rank correlation vs next-day load residual, null by shuffling days in 7-day blocks
**Expected:** the planted feature rejects H0 · **Tolerance:** α = 0.05 after Holm correction, the skill's default
**Skills:** significance-check

### TC-21: Pure-noise features are all dropped
**Pipeline:** PL-06 · **Covers:** FEAT-03 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/features/TC-21/
**Source:** generator `noise_features(days=2000, regions=50, k=20)` on seeds 12–31; same H0, statistic and null as TC-20
**Expected:** no feature rejects H0 after Holm correction · **Tolerance:** at most 1 of the 20 seeds may reject, since the family-wise error rate is α = 0.05
**Skills:** significance-check
```

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def run_tournaments(*, runs: Path = Path("runs"), out: Path = Path("tournaments"),
                    progress: Callable[[str], None] | None = None) -> tuple[TournamentGrid, ...]:
    records = list_runs(root=runs, complete_only=True)
    pending = pending_runs(records, out)
    return play_pending(pending, out, progress)
```

## PL-05: Watch a fresh pairing

**Purpose:** Watch any saved predator play any saved prey, or either one play the other side's scripted bot, on a fresh tank of your choosing, in the same replay window.
**Covers:** REPLAY-10, REPLAY-11, HUNT-15

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `list_runs` | store.listing | runs root → RunRecord | PL-02 as built | REPLAY-11 |

| 8 | `rebuild_game` | replay.rebuild | Env, TestGame → ReplayGame | PL-03 as built | REPLAY-11 |
| 9 | `show` | replay.window | ReplayGame, speed → ShowLog | PL-03 as built | REPLAY-10 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def run_match(env: str, picks: Mapping[str, str], *, seed: int, speed: int = 10,
              runs: Path = Path("runs")) -> ShowLog:
    records = list_runs(root=runs)
    chosen = find_picks(records, picks)
    game = open_match(env, chosen, seed=seed)
    return show(game, speed=speed)
```

## Open questions
- With neither `--game` nor `--seed`, saved game 0 plays, and the contract puts that default in `run_replay`; the entry may hold no branch, so it belongs to the step that reads `index`. (decided 2026-10-18: the contract now says it: `open_game` takes at most one of `index` and `seed`, neither meaning saved game 0, both a `ValueError`; `run_replay` passes both through unchanged)
- The correction counts every comparison in an environment's report, but baselines, the ladder and the trend tests are tested in three steps. (assumed: each step passes the skill that environment's full planned count, so no environment's count depends on another's, AD-14; predator-prey's is the contract's 28)
- `core` and `cli` own no step: `core` holds only the types and protocols every step names, and `cli.main` is the trigger that parses each command into its entry's arguments and sends it there. (assumed: neither needs a step)
- UR-36 accepted entry modules under `src/rllab/pipelines/`, but the contract already makes `train.train_run` and `compare.build_report` the entries for PL-01 and PL-02, and each component runs one pipeline. (decided 2026-10-18: each entry stays in its own component's `pipeline.py`; `replay` now runs two, so PL-05's entry is `replay/pipelines/pl05_match.py` and PL-03's `pipeline.py` stays)
- RUN-09 has no step of its own: it holds because Snake's and the tank's rows keep their seed streams, setup labels and file formats, and it is proven by saved-Snake-run and saved-tank-run cases. (assumed: `test-planning` adds the tank case; no workflow needed)
- `registry.make_agents` takes `init_seeds` per side (AD-16), but no contract type carries AD-05's streams from the run seed to it and to `train_loop`. (routed to `architecture-design`: add `### train.RunStreams: type` holding the training game seeds, each side's move-choice, batch and init streams and the opponent stream; until then `dt lint` reports `RunStreams` under `step-io-types`)
- `registry.make_schedule` and `colearn.make_schedule` share a function name, so PL-01 names the second step `colearn.make_schedule` to keep step IDs unique. (assumed)
- `open_match` needs a setup for `make_env`, but `run_match` takes only the environment. (assumed: the first picked run's saved setup, or `{"side": "predator"}` when both sides are scripted; every predator-prey setup builds the same `HuntEnv`)

### TC-21: Double DQN beats random and beats or ties the scripted bot
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** EVAL-04, EVAL-07, QR-01 · **Kind:** model evaluation · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-21/

**Expected:** on the held-out test seeds 0–99 (never trained on), `dqn-double` `beats` `random` and `beats` or `ties` `scripted` through PL-02; every run finishes under 60 minutes · **Tolerance:** α = 0.05 after correction over the report's 17 comparisons, as TC-12; time as measured in `curve.json`

### TC-22: Tank rules hold on every step of three kinds of play
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** TANK-01–TANK-03, TANK-05–TANK-10, BASE-06, DATA-01 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-22/
**Source:** the spec's rules (TANK-01–TANK-10 and their edge cases) and AD-13's step order; generator `tank_games(fish=[1, 5], view=["short", "wide"], game_seeds=1000–1049, rng_seed=3)` playing `baselines.RandomSwimmer`, `baselines.FleeBot` and a test-only `chaser` that swims at 2.5 toward the ball's centre (it gets removed often, so removals are exercised); positions read from each `Frame`'s `ring`, `ball` and `fish` shapes
**Expected:** at `reset`: one `tank` ring of radius 10, one `ball` circle of radius 0.5 at (0, 0), exactly `fish` fish circles of radius 0.25, each centre between 5 and 9.75 from (0, 0), all acting. On every step: the ball's centre stays within 9.5 of (0, 0) and its path this step is 0.2 long (speed 2 per second); a step with no bounce moves it in a straight line, and at a bounce its velocity leaves as `v − 2(v·n)n` about the wall normal `n` at the contact point; no fish centre is ever more than 9.75 from (0, 0); a fish is removed on a step exactly when its centre ends that step within 0.75 of the ball's, every such fish in the same step, and two fish closer than 0.5 to each other both stay; a removed fish has `rewards == 0` and `ended` on that step, never acts again, and is absent from every later frame; every other acting fish gets `rewards == 0.1`; the game is `done` with `ending == "all_removed"` exactly when the last fish goes, else with `ending == "time_cap"` at step 600 with every remaining fish `ended`; `score` == the mean over all fish of 0.1 × the steps each survived (a fish removed at the end of step k survived k steps; one never removed, 600). FleeBot fish that reach the wall stay on it at 9.75. At least one game ends `all_removed`, one `time_cap`, and at least one step removes two fish at once · **Tolerance:** 1e-9 absolute on positions, distances and rewards, float64 geometry; counts, flags and endings exact; the at-least-one clauses stop the properties passing vacuously

### TC-23: The spec's swim examples move as stated
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** TANK-04, TANK-05 · **Kind:** closed form · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-23/
**Source:** the spec's examples: TANK-04 ("chooses velocity (1, 0) … moves 0.1 units in the x direction"; "(3, 4) … swims at 2.5 units per second in direction (0.6, 0.8) and moves 0.25 units"), TANK-05 ("touching the wall that swims straight outward … stays inside the tank, touching the wall") and the edge case "(0, 0) → it stays where it is"; a 1-fish `TankEnv("short")` reset to each game seed 1000–1019, the fish's position read from the frame
**Expected:** from the start spot: move (1, 0) displaces the fish by (0.1, 0); then (3, 4) by 0.25 in direction (0.6, 0.8); then (0, 0) by nothing; then the fish swims straight outward at (2.5 × its unit position vector) until its centre is 9.75 from (0, 0), and one more outward move leaves it at 9.75. Seeds where the ball removes the fish first are skipped, and at least 15 of the 20 must run to the end · **Tolerance:** 1e-9 absolute, float64 arithmetic on exact decimal inputs; the 15-of-20 floor keeps the case from passing on skips

### TC-24: Each fish sees exactly what lies within its view
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** TANK-07, TANK-11, TANK-12 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-24/
**Source:** TANK-11, TANK-12 and their examples, the tie rule in the TANK edge cases, `tank.View`'s order; the games of TC-22 (`tank_games`, seeds 1000–1049, all three players), recomputing each fish's view from the frame and the previous frame
**Expected:** every acting fish's `obs` row has 11 values in `View` order. The ball is seen exactly when its centre is within the view radius (4 short, 20 wide) of the fish's centre; then `BALL_X, BALL_Y` == ball centre − fish centre and `BALL_VX, BALL_VY` == ball velocity − the fish's velocity over the step just run ((0, 0) at `reset`). The wall is seen exactly when `10 − |p|` ≤ the radius; then `WALL_X, WALL_Y` == the nearest wall point − `p` == `p/|p| · (10 − |p|)`. The nearest other fish still swimming is seen when within the radius, ties to the lower fish number; `FISH_X, FISH_Y` == its centre − the fish's. Every unseen item's numbers are 0 and its `*_SEEN` 0; every `*_SEEN` is 0.0 or 1.0; removed fish are never seen; under the wide view ball and wall are always seen, and the nearest fish whenever another swims. The spec's four examples each occur at least once: short view with the ball 2.9–3.1 away (seen), 4.9–5.1 away (unseen), a fish within 0.5 of the centre (wall unseen), any 1-fish game (other fish unseen) · **Tolerance:** 1e-5 absolute, `obs` is float32; flags exact

### TC-25: Same seed and swims give the same tank game
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** TANK-03, TANK-13 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-25/

### TC-26: Random swimmer spreads its swims evenly
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** BASE-04 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-26/
**Source:** BASE-04's two examples; 2,000 `act` calls on a 5-fish start obs (10,000 moves) with `np.random.default_rng(3)`, half with `explore=True`, half `False`
**Expected:** each quarter of the circle (by direction angle, from 0) holds 23%–27% of the moves; the mean speed is 1.2–1.3; every speed is within [0, 2.5]; the draws ignore `explore`; `scores` is `None` · **Tolerance:** the spec's own bands; each is about 4.6 standard errors wide for a share (√(0.25·0.75/10,000) ≈ 0.0043) and 6.9 for the mean speed (2.5/√12/√10,000 ≈ 0.0072)

### TC-27: Flee bot swims straight away from a seen ball, else stays
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** BASE-05, BASE-06 · **Kind:** closed form · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-27/
**Source:** BASE-05's example (ball seen at relative position (1, 0) → (−2.5, 0)), BASE-06's (ball unseen → (0, 0)); generator `view_rows(n=1000, rng_seed=4)`: rows with `BALL_SEEN` 0 or 1, ball position nonzero, every other number random in [−10, 10]
**Expected:** the two spec rows give exactly (−2.5, 0) and (0, 0); every seen row gives `−2.5 · (BALL_X, BALL_Y) / |(BALL_X, BALL_Y)|`, every unseen row (0, 0); changing any number other than `BALL_SEEN`, `BALL_X`, `BALL_Y` changes no move; `scores` is `None` · **Tolerance:** 1e-6 absolute, float32 output

### TC-28: Every policy-gradient rung finds a one-step target swim
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** PG-01–PG-04, PG-07, PG-08 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-28/
**Source:** known by construction: a test-only one-step game whose obs is a constant row of 11 ones and whose reward is `−|a − (1, −1)|²` for the sampled move `a`, every transition `done`; its best swim is (1, −1). `PGAgent` for each of `pg-reinforce`, `pg-baseline`, `pg-actor-critic`, `pg-ppo` with `registry.default_params`, `steps_per_update` 16 where it applies, torch seed 0, move rng `default_rng(0)`, 4,000 games
**Expected:** for every rung, `act(explore=False)` on the obs row is within 0.2 of (1, −1) in each axis; two such calls agree exactly; `scores` lies in [e^−5, e^1] throughout · **Tolerance:** 0.2 per axis, about one spread of a policy that has narrowed from its start; the build's tuning may not loosen it

### TC-29: Each rung weighs a move as its textbook formula says
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** PG-01–PG-04, PG-08 · **Kind:** closed form · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-29/
**Source:** Sutton & Barto, *Reinforcement Learning* (2nd ed., 2018), §13.3 REINFORCE `G_t = Σ_k γ^k r_{t+k}`, §13.4 with baseline `G_t − V(s_t)`, §13.5 one-step actor-critic `r_t + γ V(s_{t+1}) − V(s_t)` with no next term when `done`; Schulman et al. 2017, *Proximal Policy Optimization*, eq. 7, `min(ρA, clip(ρ, 1−ε, 1+ε)A)`; the diagonal Gaussian log density `Σ_i [−½((a_i − μ_i)/σ_i)² − log σ_i − ½ log 2π]`. The spec's examples: PG-02 "return 4.0 from a situation estimated at 3.0 … weight is 1.0"; PG-04 "chance has already risen past 1 + the clip range … pushes the policy no further". Inputs: one recorded 1-fish game of 12 steps (removed at the end) and one 5-fish batch, with γ 0.9, clip 0.2, passes 4, minibatch 8, torch seed 0
**Expected:** each rung's per-move weights equal the formula on the recorded rewards and the critic's own estimates, a removed fish's last move with no next term; the loss's log-probabilities equal the Gaussian density at the sampled move (before the cap) from the policy's mean and clamped spread; the PG-02 example gives 1.0; a training step of the critic lowers its squared error to the seen returns, so an estimate below them rises; a PPO move with ratio > 1 + ε and positive weight, or < 1 − ε and negative weight, contributes zero gradient; one PPO batch runs exactly `passes` passes, each over every move once in minibatches of `minibatch` · **Tolerance:** 1e-6 relative, float32 on short sums; zero gradients exactly 0; pass counts exact

### TC-30: The policy learns when, and from whom, its rung says
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** PG-01, PG-03, PG-06, PG-07 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-30/
**Source:** PG-01 ("after each game"), PG-03 ("after every fixed number of steps"), PG-06 and its two examples, PG-07 and its example, the PG edge case "the situation after its removal contributes nothing", `core.Learner`'s rule that `observe` is the only place learning happens; recorded transitions of 5-fish games from `tank_games` (seed 1000, FleeBot moves) fed to each rung's `observe` with `steps_per_update` 8, torch seed 0, and a fixed probe obs set of 20 rows
**Expected:** `scores` and `act(explore=False)` on the probe set change only after the `observe` that ends the last fish's experience (`pg-reinforce`, `pg-baseline`) or completes step 8k (`pg-actor-critic`, `pg-ppo`), never between; `act` on 5 rows equals `act` on each row alone, for `explore=False` and for `explore=True` with the same rng state per row order; changing any one fish's rewards changes the next update; changing a `done` transition's `next_obs` changes nothing; a test game played twice from the same seed is identical; with `explore=True` the same rng seed gives the same move and two seeds give different ones · **Tolerance:** exact, determinism on CPU with one thread (AD-05); "changes" means any probe value differs

### TC-31: A tank run trains, tests and saves like a Snake run
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** RUN-01–RUN-03, EVAL-01, EVAL-02, BASE-07, PG-06, DATA-01 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-31/
**Source:** RUN-01–RUN-03, EVAL-01, EVAL-02 (tank clauses), AD-05, AD-11; `train_run` twice for `pg-ppo` with fish 5, view `short`, seed 1, 3,000 steps, 4 curve points, inside an env wrapper that records every `reset` seed and an agent wrapper that records every `act` and `observe`; plus `random` and `scripted` baseline records on fish 5, view `wide`
**Expected:** the run's folder sits under `tank/fish-5-short/`, the baselines' under `tank/fish-5-wide/`; training resets use only seeds ≥ 1,000; each test round resets seeds 0–99 in order; each `CurvePoint.score` == the mean of its 100 games' `score`; each training step makes one `act(explore=True)` on all acting rows and then one `observe` per acting fish in fish order, none for a fish after its removal; the two runs match as in TC-09 (settings, curve, test games, loaded agents' `scores`), `elapsed_s` and folder name excluded; reloaded `test_games.jsonl` moves equal the played float32 moves bit for bit; each baseline tests once with `train_steps == 0` · **Tolerance:** exact for seeds, call order, files and moves; scores 1e-9 relative, mean of 100 floats

### TC-32: 5-fish short-view PPO beats the random swimmer and beats or ties the flee bot
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** EVAL-04, EVAL-07, QR-01 · **Kind:** model evaluation · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-32/
**Source:** EVAL-07 (the tank's top learner is 5-fish, short-view PPO), UR-58; real runs of `pg-ppo` with fish 5 and view `short` on training seeds 1–5 with the build's tuned settings, plus `random` and `scripted` on the same setup
**Expected:** on the held-out test seeds 0–99 (never trained on), `pg-ppo` `beats` `random` and `beats` or `ties` `scripted` through PL-02's tank report; every tank run finishes under 60 minutes · **Tolerance:** α = 0.05 after correction over the tank report's own comparison count (AD-14); time as measured in `curve.json`
**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing

### TC-33: Tank comparisons pair only like setups and stay in the tank's report
**Pipeline:** PL-02 · **Covers:** EVAL-05, PG-05, BASE-07, RUN-09 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-33/
**Source:** EVAL-05's five tank pairs, PG-05 and its example, BASE-07 and its example, AD-12, AD-14; generator `fake_runs(seed=15)` writing 5 complete tank runs for each of: the four rungs at fish 1 short, `pg-ppo` at fish 5 short, `pg-ppo` at fish 5 wide, with `pg-actor-critic` given `lr` 0.0005 against the others' 0.001; `random` and `scripted` records for fish 1 short and fish 5 short only; plus the Snake runs of TC-15
**Expected:** `reports/tank/report.md` holds the ladder pairs REINFORCE → baseline, actor-critic → PPO, 1-fish → 5-fish and short → wide as comparisons, and baseline → actor-critic in `waiting` naming `lr`; 5-fish wide PPO's baseline comparisons are in `waiting` naming the missing fish-5-wide baselines, never made against fish-5-short ones; every tank comparison's `env` is `tank`; `reports/snake/report.md` is the same file with and without the tank runs present · **Tolerance:** exact

### TC-34: Tank scores: a planted gap beats, the same distribution ties
**Pipeline:** PL-02 · **Covers:** EVAL-04 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-34/
**Source:** generator `fake_tank_runs`: (a) seed 16, learner 5 seeds × 100 test games with game score ~ Uniform(30, 50), baseline 100 games ~ Uniform(5, 15), same test seeds 0–99; (b) seeds 17–36, both ~ Uniform(10, 30). Given to `null-hypothesis-testing` as in TC-12, with the tank report's own comparison count: H0 equal mean game score, H1 they differ, α 0.05 after correction
**Expected:** (a) rejects H0 → `beats`; (b) `ties` · **Tolerance:** (a) must reject, a 30-second gap is many standard errors; (b) at most 1 of the 20 seeds may reject, the family-wise error rate is α = 0.05
**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing

### TC-35: The tank is done only when 5-fish short-view PPO clears the bar
**Pipeline:** PL-02 · **Covers:** EVAL-06, EVAL-07 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-35/
**Source:** EVAL-07's two tank examples; `write_report` on recorded tank `Comparison`s: (a) 5-fish short PPO beats `random`, ties `scripted` with a non-empty explanation, every other tank comparison made and every tie and no-difference explained; (b) as (a) but 5-fish wide PPO beats `scripted` while 5-fish short PPO loses to it; (c) as (a) with the 1-fish → 5-fish step in `waiting`
**Expected:** (a) the tank report says done; (b) not done, `not_done` names 5-fish short PPO's loss to `scripted`; (c) not done, `not_done` names the waiting step · **Tolerance:** exact

### TC-36: Every saved tank game replays as it was scored
**Pipeline:** PL-03 · **Covers:** REPLAY-01, REPLAY-06, REPLAY-08, REPLAY-09 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-36/
**Source:** REPLAY-06, REPLAY-08, REPLAY-09, AD-07; runs from `train_run`: `pg-ppo` fish 5 short, seed 1, 3,000 steps; `scripted` fish 5 wide; `random` fish 1 short
**Expected:** for every saved game, `open_game(index=i)` gives no `ReplayMismatch`, `moves`, `scores`, `score_after` and `ending` equal to the saved game, and one more frame than moves; every frame's labels hold the time in seconds (0.1 × step), the fish still swimming, and one removal time per fish removed so far; each swimming fish's circle carries its `agent`, removed ones are absent; the PPO game's `scores` give one spread per swimming fish per step, the baselines' are `None`; tampering one saved `score_after` raises `ReplayMismatch` at that step · **Tolerance:** exact, replays rebuild the same floats

### TC-37: Snake runs saved before the tank reproduce, compare and replay identically
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** RUN-09, RUN-03, REPLAY-06 · **Kind:** regression · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-37/
**Source:** RUN-09 and pipelines assumption 6 (UR-66); a snapshot of Snake run folders made by the accepted S1 build at the S1 exit, before any S2 code lands: `tabular` / `plain` and `dqn-double` / `plain`, seed 1, 2,000 steps, 4 curve points, plus the `scripted` baseline, with `reports/snake/report.md` built from them; stored under the fixture's `snapshot/`, or by path plus sha256 if over 200 KB
**Expected:** with the tank code present: `list_runs` reads all three as complete with unchanged `RunSettings`; rerunning each from its saved `settings.json` gives the snapshot's `settings.json`, curve `steps` and `score`, and `test_games.jsonl` byte for byte; the snapshot's saved agents load and give the same `scores` on every obs in their test games; `open_game` replays every saved game with no `ReplayMismatch`; `rllab compare` on the snapshot alone gives the same Snake comparisons (outcome, test, `p_adjusted`) as the snapshot report · **Tolerance:** exact; `elapsed_s` and folder names excluded, they're wall-clock

## Open questions
- The `null-hypothesis-testing` skill installed here is a stub, so the H0, statistic (difference of mean food), null (label permutation within each test seed) and correction in TC-12, TC-13 and TC-21 are written down as what the skill is given; AD-08 lets the real skill pick the test and correction. (assumed: the cases keep their planted / pure-noise expectations whatever test the skill picks)
- SNAKE-04 doesn't say whether the head may move into the cell the tail is leaving on the same move. No case pins it; TC-01 checks only that a collision follows a danger fact. (decided 2026-10-18: stays unpinned for now, UR-43)
- The `--smoke` flag is not in `cli.main`'s contract signature. (decided 2026-10-18: a test-only flag the wiring task adds, not in the contract, UR-42)
- TC-21 trains real runs (5 × up to 60 minutes) and is the stage's acceptance check, run by hand, not in the fast test run. (decided 2026-10-18: a manual acceptance check, UR-44)
- TC-29 tests each rung's weights, log-probabilities, PPO clip and pass count at one step, but `pg.PGAgent`'s contract exposes only `act`, `scores`, `observe`, `save` and `load`. (decided 2026-10-18: routed to `architecture-design` to add the interface, UR-73; until it lands TC-29's tester can only work through whole updates)
- PG-01 and PG-03 ask for "hand-computed" values. TC-29 states them as the textbook formulas the test evaluates on recorded inputs, not as numbers. (decided 2026-10-18: formulas only, UR-68)
- TANK-10 vs TANK-09: a fish removed at the end of step k is scored as surviving 0.1·k seconds, while its rewards sum to 0.1·(k−1), since the removal step pays 0. (decided 2026-10-18: yes, UR-69)
- TANK-11 leaves three readings open: "within the view radius" is centre to centre; the ball's relative velocity subtracts the fish's velocity over the step just run ((0, 0) at reset); the wall's nearest point is on radius 10, not 9.75. (decided 2026-10-18: all three as TC-24 states, UR-70; a fish exactly at (0, 0) under the wide view stays untested, UR-71)
- TC-37's snapshot can only be made at the S1 exit, by the accepted S1 build. (decided 2026-10-18: its `snapshot/` files and hashes are added then, each write approved by the user, UR-72; until then TC-37 is a planned case with no data)

=== TOUR-01
<!-- docs/spec.md:479 -->
- **TOUR-01** Each ladder run (rungs 1 to 3) saves 10 versions of each side at evenly spaced points through training, the last at the end.
  - Given a finished rung-2 run, when its saved versions are listed, then there are 10 predator and 10 prey versions, the last of each equal to the final agent.
=== TOUR-02
<!-- docs/spec.md:481 -->
- **TOUR-02** Every pairing of a run's predator version i with its prey version j, for i and j from 1 to 10, plays the same 20 held-out test tanks, and is scored as the predator's catches per game (HUNT-11).
  - Given a finished ladder run, when its tournament is played, then all 100 pairings have a score, each from the same 20 test seeds, none of them used in training.
=== TOUR-03
<!-- docs/spec.md:483 -->
- **TOUR-03** Each rung has one tournament chart: a 10 × 10 grid of predator version against prey version, each cell the mean over the rung's 5 seed runs of that pairing's score; the chart follows the `dataviz` skill.
  - Given 5 complete seed runs of a rung, when its chart is opened, then it shows 100 cells, each the mean of 5 scores.
  - Given fewer than 5 complete seed runs of a rung, when its chart is requested, then it is not made, and the report lists the missing seeds.
=== QR-01
<!-- docs/spec.md:495 -->
- **QR-01** Each training run in any environment, including its test games, finishes in under 1 hour (60 minutes) on the laptop's processor; a predator-prey run trains both its learning sides within that hour, and its tournament games are not part of the run.
  - Checked by the elapsed time saved with each run; RUN-07 stops any run that reaches 60 minutes.
=== REPLAY-10
<!-- docs/spec.md:284 -->
- **REPLAY-10** A predator-prey replay shows the tank, the predator, every prey still swimming, the pellets, the time in seconds, the pellets eaten so far, the prey caught so far, and the time each caught prey was caught.
  - Given a predator-prey replay at any step, when it is shown, then all of these are visible; at the end, the ending (every prey caught, or the 60-second cap) is shown.
=== REPLAY-11
<!-- docs/spec.md:286 -->
- **REPLAY-11** A fresh predator-prey game can pair any saved predator with any saved prey, or either one with the other side's scripted bot.
  - Given a predator saved by one run, prey saved by another run and a seed, when the game is watched twice, then both games are identical.
=== HUNT-15
<!-- docs/spec.md:432 -->
- **HUNT-15** The same game seed and the same moves always give the same game.
  - Given a game seed and a list of moves for the predator and every prey, when the game is played twice, then every position, catch, pellet, reward and ending is identical.

**Last updated:** 2026-10-18

*Written by `dt roadmap` from doc statuses and todo checkboxes; never hand-edited.

## S1: Snake + shared core
**Outline:** Train table-based Q-learning, then DQN built one piece at a time, on Snake in under an hour on the laptop, compare it with random and scripted baselines, watch a replay, and explain the result.
**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing, dataviz

## M1: Train a run
**Outline:** `rllab train` trains the table learner or any of the four DQN rungs, or records a baseline, on plain or shaped Snake from one seed, and saves a complete run folder.
**Skills:** —
**References:** —
**Exit:** Every learner and baseline trains to a complete run folder on either reward; `rllab train --learner tabular --reward plain --seed 1 --smoke` passes; TC-01–TC-11 pass.
**Plan:** [../tasks/plan.md](../tasks/plan.md)

## M2: Compare and replay
**Outline:** `rllab compare` reports every baseline and ladder comparison with learning-curve charts and the done check, `rllab replay` shows any saved or fresh game with the agent's move scores, and the DQN defaults are tuned to the 60-minute limit.
**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing, dataviz
**References:** —
**Exit:** TC-12–TC-20 pass, and TC-21 holds by hand: Double DQN beats random and beats or ties the scripted bot, each run under 60 minutes.

## S2: Fish tank
**Outline:** Train policy-gradient fish, from REINFORCE up to 5-fish PPO, in under an hour per run in the circular tank, compare them with a random swimmer and a scripted fleeing bot, and watch any school of fish as a replay.
**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing, dataviz

### Predator-prey environment (`HUNT`)
**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 5
**Stage:** S3
**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
**Behavior:** The fish tank's circle, with a learning predator in place of the ball, 5 prey and 10 food pellets. Every tenth of a second the predator and each prey still swimming choose how to swim. A prey touching a pellet eats it, and the pellet reappears elsewhere; the predator touching a prey removes it. Everyone sees only what lies within the short view radius. Prey are rewarded for pellets, the predator for catches.

**Requirements**
- **HUNT-01** The tank is a circle of radius 10 holding one predator, 5 prey and 10 pellets.
  - Given any game, when it starts, then the tank's radius is 10 and it holds exactly one predator, 5 prey and 10 pellets.
  - Given any step of any game, when the pellets are counted, then there are 10.
- **HUNT-02** Each game starts with the predator at the centre, each prey at a seed-chosen spot between 5 units from the centre and the wall, and each pellet at a seed-chosen spot at least 2 units from every prey.
  - Given any game, when it starts, then the predator is at the centre, every prey is between 5 and 9.75 units from the centre, and every pellet is at least 2 units from every prey.
  - Given the same game seed twice, when both games start, then every prey and pellet spot is identical.
- **HUNT-03** Every 0.1 seconds of tank time, the predator and each prey still swimming choose a swim velocity as in TANK-04, capped at 3.0 units per second for the predator and 2.5 for a prey.
  - Given the predator choosing velocity (3, 4), when the step runs, then it swims at 3.0 units per second in direction (0.6, 0.8) and moves 0.3 units.
  - Given a prey choosing velocity (3, 4), when the step runs, then it swims at 2.5 units per second and moves 0.25 units.
- **HUNT-04** No fish leaves the tank: a move that would carry the predator (radius 0.5) or a prey (radius 0.25) past the wall stops it at the wall.
  - Given the predator touching the wall and swimming straight outward, when the step runs, then it stays inside the tank, touching the wall.
- **HUNT-05** Prey swim through each other unharmed, and the predator swims through pellets without effect.
  - Given two prey whose paths cross, when the step runs, then both finish their moves and both are still swimming.
  - Given the predator passing over a pellet, when the step ends, then the pellet is still there.
- **HUNT-06** At the end of each step, the predator catches every prey whose centre is within 0.75 units of its own; a caught prey is removed, takes no more moves and is no longer seen.
  - Given a prey whose centre ends a step 0.7 units from the predator's, when the step ends, then it is caught.
  - Given a prey whose centre ends a step 0.8 units from the predator's, when the step ends, then it is still swimming.
  - Given a caught prey, when the other fish's views are read, then none of them sees it.
- **HUNT-07** At the end of each step, after catches, each prey still swimming eats every pellet whose centre is within 0.5 units of its own, and each eaten pellet reappears at once at a seed-chosen spot at least 2 units from every prey still swimming.
  - Given a prey whose centre ends a step 0.4 units from a pellet, when the step ends, then the pellet is eaten, a new pellet stands at least 2 units from every prey, and there are still 10 pellets.
  - Given a prey 0.6 units from a pellet, when the step ends, then the pellet is not eaten.
- **HUNT-08** A game ends when all 5 prey are caught, or at 60 seconds of tank time (600 steps).
  - Given 4 prey caught, when the steps continue, then the game goes on until the last prey is caught or 600 steps have run.
- **HUNT-09** A prey's reward for a step is +1 for each pellet it eats in that step and 0 otherwise; the step in which it is caught pays 0 and ends its experience.
  - Given a prey that eats one pellet in a step, when the step ends, then its reward is +1.
  - Given a prey caught during a step, when the step ends, then its reward is 0 and it has no further steps.
- **HUNT-10** The predator's reward for a step is +1 for each prey it catches in that step and 0 otherwise.
  - Given the predator catching 2 prey in one step, when the step ends, then its reward is +2.
- **HUNT-11** A game's prey score is the pellets eaten per prey: all pellets eaten by the 5 prey, caught ones included, divided by 5. Its predator score is the number of prey caught, from 0 to 5.
  - Given 5 prey that eat 3, 0, 5, 2 and 0 pellets, when the game is scored, then the prey score is 2.0.
  - Given 3 prey caught by the end, when the game is scored, then the predator score is 3.
- **HUNT-12** At every step, each prey sees exactly 14 numbers about what lies within its view radius: for the predator, whether it is seen, its position relative to the prey (2 numbers) and its velocity relative to the prey (2); for the wall, whether it is seen and the offset to its nearest point (2); for the nearest other prey, whether it is seen and its relative position (2); for the nearest pellet, whether it is seen and its relative position (2). Anything beyond the radius is seen as no, and its numbers are 0.
  - Given the predator 3 units from a prey, when the prey's view is read, then the predator is seen as yes and its relative position and velocity are the true ones.
  - Given the predator 5 units from a prey, when the prey's view is read, then the predator is seen as no and its 4 numbers are 0.
- **HUNT-13** At every step, the predator sees exactly 11 numbers about what lies within its view radius: for the wall, whether it is seen and the offset to its nearest point (2); for the nearest prey, whether it is seen, its relative position (2) and its relative velocity (2); for the nearest pellet, whether it is seen and its relative position (2). Anything beyond the radius is seen as no, and its numbers are 0.
  - Given the nearest prey 3 units from the predator, when the predator's view is read, then it is seen as yes with its true relative position and velocity.
  - Given every prey caught but the game not yet over, when the predator's view is read, then the nearest prey is seen as no and its 4 numbers are 0.
- **HUNT-14** The predator and every prey have the same view radius: 4 units, the short view of TANK-12.
  - Given a predator and a prey 3.9 units apart, when both views are read, then each sees the other; at 4.1 units apart, neither does.
- **HUNT-15** The same game seed and the same moves always give the same game.
  - Given a game seed and a list of moves for the predator and every prey, when the game is played twice, then every position, catch, pellet, reward and ending is identical.

**Edge cases & errors**
- Two prey or two pellets are exactly equally near → the fish sees the one with the lower number.
- A prey is caught in the step it reaches a pellet → catches are settled first, so it is removed and eats nothing.
- Two prey are within 0.5 units of the same pellet → the lower-numbered prey eats it.
- The predator catches several prey in one step → all are removed, and its reward counts each one.

### Co-learning ladder (`COEV`)
**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 3
**Stage:** S3
**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
**Behavior:** The prey share one PPO policy, as the fish tank's 5 fish did; the predator has its own. Because each side's opponent changes while it learns, the ladder adds one fix for a moving opponent per rung, so each fix's effect can be measured:
1. Both learn at once: plain co-learning, expected to chase or cycle.
2. Take turns: one side is frozen while the other trains, swapping every 20 updates.
3. Past versions: each side trains against a pool of the other side's saved past versions.

Two controls each train one side against the other side's scripted bot, to measure what a moving opponent costs that side. Three rungs and two controls make 5 setups.

**Requirements**
- **COEV-01** The predator has its own policy, which learns with the PPO update of PG-04 from the predator's experience only.
  - Given a ladder run, when the predator's policy is updated, then it learns only from the predator's moves and rewards.
- **COEV-02** In rung 1, both policies learn from the same games at every update.
  - Given a rung-1 run, when any update is applied, then both the predator's and the prey's policies change.
- **COEV-03** In rung 2, one side learns while the other is frozen, swapping every 20 updates of the learning side, starting with the predator.
  - Given a rung-2 run, when its first 20 updates run, then only the predator's policy changes; during the next 20, only the prey's does.
- **COEV-04** In rung 3, each side saves a copy of its policy every 20 of its updates into a pool that keeps its last 10 copies, and each training game pits a learning side against a copy drawn uniformly at random from the other side's pool, its latest copy included; the drawn copy does not learn.
  - Given a side that has saved 11 copies, when its pool is read, then it holds the 10 latest.
  - Given a pool of 10 copies and 10,000 draws, when the draws are counted, then each copy is drawn between 9% and 11% of the time.
- **COEV-05** Each control trains one side against the other side's scripted bot: the predator against the scripted forager, or the prey against the scripted chaser, with every other setting the same as rung 1. Both controls are run, so predator-prey has 5 setups (3 rungs, 2 controls) and 25 runs at 5 seeds each.
  - Given a predator control run, when it trains, then every prey move comes from the scripted forager and only the predator's policy changes.
  - Given a prey control run, when it trains, then every predator move comes from the scripted chaser and only the prey's policy changes.
- **COEV-06** Each predator-prey setup differs from the one it is compared with only by the piece it adds: rung 2 from rung 1 by taking turns, rung 3 from rung 2 by the pool, and rung 1 from each control by the learning opponent.
  - Given the saved settings of any two setups compared in EVAL-05, when they are compared, then the only differences are the added piece and its own settings.

**Edge cases & errors**
- Rung 3 starts before a side has saved a copy → that side's pool holds its starting policy.
- One side stops winning anything (the predator never catches, or the prey are always caught at once) → the run keeps going and saves its curves; the comparison reports the result, and the problem is visible in the curves and the tournament.

### Version tournament (`TOUR`)
**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 3
**Stage:** S3
**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
**Behavior:** Each ladder run saves versions of both sides as it trains. Every predator version then plays every prey version on held-out tanks, and one chart per rung shows how catches change as each side gets older, so the learner can see whether later predators beat earlier prey (and the other way round), or whether the two sides cycle.

**Requirements**
- **TOUR-01** Each ladder run (rungs 1 to 3) saves 10 versions of each side at evenly spaced points through training, the last at the end.
  - Given a finished rung-2 run, when its saved versions are listed, then there are 10 predator and 10 prey versions, the last of each equal to the final agent.
- **TOUR-02** Every pairing of a run's predator version i with its prey version j, for i and j from 1 to 10, plays the same 20 held-out test tanks, and is scored as the predator's catches per game (HUNT-11).
  - Given a finished ladder run, when its tournament is played, then all 100 pairings have a score, each from the same 20 test seeds, none of them used in training.
- **TOUR-03** Each rung has one tournament chart: a 10 × 10 grid of predator version against prey version, each cell the mean over the rung's 5 seed runs of that pairing's score; the chart follows the `dataviz` skill.
  - Given 5 complete seed runs of a rung, when its chart is opened, then it shows 100 cells, each the mean of 5 scores.
  - Given fewer than 5 complete seed runs of a rung, when its chart is requested, then it is not made, and the report lists the missing seeds.
- **TOUR-04** Each rung's tournament is tested for a trend with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill. It is given the 100 pairing scores of each of the rung's 5 seed runs. The tournament shows a trend when catches rise with the predator's version and fall with the prey's version, each significant at 5% after correcting for the number of tests in the report; otherwise it shows no trend, which, like a tie, needs a written explanation (EVAL-06), such as a cycle.
  - Given scores that rise with predator version and fall with prey version in every seed, when the test runs, then the chart reports a trend, with the test's result.
  - Given scores that cycle, when the test runs, then the chart reports no trend and is marked unexplained until its explanation is written.

## Data requirements
- **DATA-01** All training and test experience comes from the agents playing in the lab's environments, Snake, the fish tank and predator-prey; no outside data is used.
  - Given any run, when its inputs are listed, then the only source of games is the run's own environment, started from the run's own seeds.

## Quality requirements
- **QR-01** Each training run in any environment, including its test games, finishes in under 1 hour (60 minutes) on the laptop's processor; a predator-prey run trains both its learning sides within that hour, and its tournament games are not part of the run.
  - Checked by the elapsed time saved with each run; RUN-07 stops any run that reaches 60 minutes.
- **QR-02** Training, testing and replay run on the Apple Silicon laptop with no NVIDIA graphics card.
  - Checked by running every learner's training, a comparison and a replay on that laptop.

## Assumptions
- One learner uses the lab on one Apple Silicon laptop, evenings and weekends.
- Each environment's full ladder takes many hours in total; only each single run has the 1-hour limit. Snake and the fish tank have 6 learner setups × 5 seeds = 30 runs each: Snake's are the table learner, 4 DQN rungs and the shaped-reward Double DQN; the tank's are the 4 policy-gradient rungs with 1 fish, PPO with 5 fish, and 5-fish PPO with the wide view. Predator-prey has 5 setups × 5 seeds = 25 runs: 3 ladder rungs and 2 controls.
- The done check in EVAL-07 judges only each environment's top learner; the other rungs are reported, and may lose to the scripted bot, but don't gate it.
- Predator-prey's lower rungs may lose, chase or cycle; that is part of the lesson, as long as it is explained.
- Learner tuning settings (learning rate, discount, exploration schedule, store size, refresh interval, steps per update, passes per batch, clip range, the policy's starting spread, each predator-prey side's training length) are saved per run but not fixed by the spec; they are found during the build within the one-hour limit.

6:Three small 2D worlds, each with a ladder of hand-written learners. Classic Snake on a fixed 10×10 board is learned by table-based Q-learning, then a deep Q-network (a learned function that scores each move) built one piece at a time, then plain versus shaped rewards. A circular fish tank, where a bouncing ball removes any fish it touches, is learned by policy-gradient learners (which learn the chance of each move directly), built from REINFORCE up to PPO on one fish, then PPO with 5 fish sharing one learner, then a wide view versus the short view. In the same tank, a learning predator hunts 5 prey that forage for food pellets, and both sides learn with PPO up a ladder of fixes for a moving opponent: both learning at once, taking turns, then training against the other side's past versions, with a scripted-opponent control for each side and a tournament of each side's saved versions against the other's. Every learner is scored against a random player and a scripted bot on the same held-out games, every added piece gets a before-and-after comparison, every run saves its evidence, and any saved agent can be watched in a replay that shows how it chose its moves. See the [brief](brief.md) for why.
11:| The learner (sole user) | Train each learner on Snake, in the fish tank or as predator and prey in under an hour, compare it with the baselines and with the previous rung of the ladder, explain each result from saved evidence, and watch any trained agent play |
14:**In scope:** Snake environment, Baselines, Table-based Q-learning, DQN ladder, Reward shaping, Training runs, Evaluation & comparison, Replay viewer, Fish tank environment, Policy-gradient ladder, Predator-prey environment, Co-learning ladder, Version tournament.
18:- Learning from pixels or from a picture of the board or tank, and Atari games; agents see only the compact hand-made facts in SNAKE-06, TANK-11, HUNT-12 and HUNT-13. A grid-view Snake learner is a possible later follow-up.
23:- A general framework beyond what Snake, the fish tank and predator-prey need.
27:- A separate learner for each fish of one kind: every fish in the fish tank shares one learner, and so does every prey.
28:- Shaped rewards outside Snake: the fish tank rewards only time survived, and predator-prey only pellets eaten and prey caught.
31:- In predator-prey, the policy-gradient rungs below PPO, two or more predators, and a predator that sees further than the prey.
71:**Behavior:** Two non-learning players in each environment set the bar every learner must clear: one picks moves at random, the other follows a simple rule. In Snake the bot heads for the food; in the tank it flees the ball. In predator-prey each side has its own pair: the scripted chaser swims at the nearest prey it sees, and the scripted forager flees the predator, or else swims to the nearest pellet. All play the same test games as the learners.
83:- **BASE-04** The random swimmer, used for the fish tank's fish, the prey and the predator, picks each move with a uniformly random direction and a speed drawn uniformly from 0 to its fish's top speed: 2.5 units per second for a tank fish or prey, 3.0 for the predator.
85:  - Given 10,000 random-swimmer moves of a tank fish or prey, when their speeds are averaged, then the mean is between 1.2 and 1.3.
86:  - Given 10,000 random-predator moves, when their speeds are averaged, then the mean is between 1.45 and 1.55.
91:- **BASE-07** In each comparison, the baselines play the same setup as the learner they are compared with: in the fish tank, the same fish count and view radius; in predator-prey, the same fixed opponent the learner is tested against (EVAL-01).
93:  - Given a learning predator, when the random predator and the scripted chaser are scored, then they play the same test tanks against the scripted forager.
94:  - Given learning prey, when the random prey and the scripted forager are scored, then they play the same test tanks against the scripted chaser.
95:- **BASE-08** When the scripted chaser sees a prey, it swims at 3.0 units per second directly toward the nearest prey it sees.
96:  - Given the nearest prey seen at relative position (1, 0), when the chaser moves, then its swim velocity is (3.0, 0).
97:- **BASE-09** When the scripted chaser sees no prey, it stays still.
98:  - Given no prey seen, when the chaser moves, then its swim velocity is (0, 0).
99:- **BASE-10** When the scripted forager sees the predator, it swims at 2.5 units per second directly away from the predator.
100:  - Given the predator seen at relative position (0, 2) and a pellet seen, when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (0, −2.5).
101:- **BASE-11** When the scripted forager doesn't see the predator but sees a pellet, it swims at 2.5 units per second directly toward the nearest pellet it sees.
102:  - Given the predator seen as no and the nearest pellet at relative position (−3, 0), when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (−2.5, 0).
103:- **BASE-12** When the scripted forager sees neither the predator nor a pellet, it stays still.
104:  - Given the predator and every pellet seen as no, when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (0, 0).
109:- The scripted forager flees into the wall → it stops at the wall (HUNT-04) and keeps swimming away from the predator while it sees it.
110:- The scripted forager is less than 2.5 units from the nearest pellet → it reaches the pellet on the step it would pass it, at most 0.25 units of swimming.
187:- **RUN-01** A training run trains one learner setup from one training seed on one environment setup: Snake with one reward (the table learner or one DQN rung), the fish tank with one fish count and one view radius (one policy-gradient rung), or predator-prey with one ladder rung (both sides learning) or one control (one side learning).
189:  - Given predator-prey rung 2 and a seed, when a run is started with one action, then it trains the predator and the prey together under that rung's rules and nothing else.
196:  - Given a predator-prey ladder run in progress, when a curve point is reached, then the display shows both the predator's and the prey's latest test score.
205:- **RUN-09** After each new environment is added, runs saved in the earlier environments still reproduce, compare and replay identically: Snake runs after the fish tank, and Snake and fish tank runs after predator-prey.
208:  - Given Snake and fish tank runs, comparisons and replays saved before predator-prey was added, when they are rerun or made again, then every curve point, result and replayed game is identical.
217:**Behavior:** Every learner and baseline is scored on the same 100 held-out test games of its environment; in predator-prey each side plays a fixed scripted opponent, never its co-learning partner. Learning curves show progress against the baselines. Comparison reports say, from 5 training seeds per learner, whether each learner beats the baselines, and what each added piece changed. Each result carries a written explanation, so a tie counts.
223:  - Given a predator-prey run, when its predator is tested, then every test game pits it against the scripted forager; when its prey are tested, then every test game pits them against the scripted chaser.
224:- **EVAL-02** An agent's test score is the mean of its game scores over the 100 test games: food eaten per game in Snake, the game score of TANK-10 (mean seconds survived per fish) in the fish tank, and in predator-prey the side's score of HUNT-11 (prey caught per game for the predator, pellets eaten per prey for the prey).
227:  - Given 100 predator-prey test games with known catches, when the predator's score is computed, then it equals the mean catches per game.
231:  - Given a finished predator-prey ladder run, when its curves are opened, then there is one curve for the predator and one for the prey, each with its own side's baseline lines.
235:- **EVAL-05** Each added piece gets a before-and-after comparison with the rung below it, made the same way as EVAL-04. In Snake: table learner → plain DQN, plain → + replay buffer, + replay buffer → + target network, + target network → + Double DQN, and plain reward → shaped reward. In the tank: REINFORCE → + learned baseline, + learned baseline → actor-critic, actor-critic → PPO (all with 1 fish and the short view), 1-fish PPO → 5-fish PPO (short view), and 5-fish PPO short view → wide view. In predator-prey, for each side: its control → rung 1 (what a moving opponent costs), rung 1 → rung 2, and rung 2 → rung 3. Every outcome counts: better, worse, or no difference.
240:- **EVAL-07** Each environment is done when its top learner beats the random player, beats or ties the scripted bot, every comparison in EVAL-04 and EVAL-05 for that environment has a result, and every tie and no-difference result is explained. Snake's top learner is the Double DQN agent; the fish tank's is 5-fish, short-view PPO. Predator-prey's top learners are both sides of rung 3, each judged against its own side's baselines, and predator-prey also needs every tournament chart (TOUR-03) to have a result, with rung 3's showing a trend or its no-trend result explained.
247:  - Given a rung-3 predator that beats the random predator and ties the scripted chaser with the tie explained, rung-3 prey that beat the random prey and the scripted forager, every predator-prey comparison made and explained, and rung 3's tournament showing a trend, when the done check runs, then it reports predator-prey done.
248:  - Given everything else done but rung 3's tournament showing no trend and no explanation, when the done check runs, then it reports predator-prey not done and names the missing explanation.
249:- **EVAL-08** Each predator-prey control comparison states, beside its result, that the control trained against the same scripted opponent it is tested against.
250:  - Given the predator control → rung 1 comparison, when the report is opened, then it says the control trained against the scripted forager, its test opponent.
251:  - Given the prey control → rung 1 comparison, when the report is opened, then it says the control trained against the scripted chaser, its test opponent.
256:- A predator-prey control run → it gives only its learning side's agent; the side that played the scripted bot has no agent to compare.
261:**Behavior:** The learner picks a saved agent and one of its test games, or a fresh game seed, and watches it play step by step. Next to the board or tank, the viewer shows how the agent chose: its score for each of Snake's 3 moves, or each fish's chosen swim and how sure it was, predator included, so its choices can be explained. In predator-prey, any saved predator can be watched against any saved prey, or against the other side's scripted bot.
281:- **REPLAY-09** At every step of a fish tank or predator-prey learner's game, each learning fish still swimming, the predator included, shows an arrow for its chosen swim velocity and a number for how sure it was: the spread of its move choice, smaller meaning surer.
283:  - Given a tank baseline replay, or a scripted side in a predator-prey replay, when it is shown, then its arrows and spreads are shown as not applicable.
284:- **REPLAY-10** A predator-prey replay shows the tank, the predator, every prey still swimming, the pellets, the time in seconds, the pellets eaten so far, the prey caught so far, and the time each caught prey was caught.
285:  - Given a predator-prey replay at any step, when it is shown, then all of these are visible; at the end, the ending (every prey caught, or the 60-second cap) is shown.
286:- **REPLAY-11** A fresh predator-prey game can pair any saved predator with any saved prey, or either one with the other side's scripted bot.
287:  - Given a predator saved by one run, prey saved by another run and a seed, when the game is watched twice, then both games are identical.
372:- **PG-06** With several fish on one side, the fish tank's 5 fish or predator-prey's 5 prey, every fish on that side uses the same policy on its own view, and every one's experience trains it.
375:  - Given a predator-prey run with the prey learning, when an update is applied, then the prey policy learns from all 5 prey up to each one's capture.
376:- **PG-07** While training, each learning fish, the predator included, picks its move at random from its policy's spread; during test games it takes the policy's most likely move.
378:- **PG-08** The learning updates of every rung, and of both predator-prey sides, are written in the project; no ready-made learning algorithm is used.
379:  - Given any trained rung or predator-prey side, when its learning steps are traced, then each one is code written in the project.
