## PL-01: Train a run
**Entry:** `src/rllab/train/pipeline.py::train_run` · **Trigger:** `rllab train [--env snake|tank|hunt] --learner L [--reward R | --fish F --view V | --match M | --side S] --seed N [--steps S]`
**Purpose:** Train one learner (or record a baseline) on Snake, the fish tank or predator-prey from one seed and save a run folder holding each learning side's curve, final agent, any saved versions and the test games.
**Covers:** SNAKE-01–SNAKE-08, TANK-01–TANK-13, HUNT-01–HUNT-15, BASE-01–BASE-12, TAB-01–TAB-04, DQN-01–DQN-04, DQN-06, DQN-07, PG-01–PG-04, PG-06–PG-08, SHAPE-01–SHAPE-04, COEV-01–COEV-05, TOUR-01, RUN-01–RUN-09, EVAL-01, EVAL-02, DATA-01, QR-01, QR-02

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `make_env` | registry.envs | env, setup → Env | name → constructor dict (`snake`, `tank`, `hunt`), each environment's setup keys and values checked (predator-prey's `match` or a baseline's `side`, all building one `HuntEnv`), `UnknownName` listing the choices | RUN-01, RUN-08 |
| 2 | `SnakeEnv` | snake.env | reward → Env | one side `snake` of one agent, 10×10 board, 3 relative moves, food from the game's own generator, wall/body/starved endings, the 11 facts, plain reward, food count as the side's float score, cells in the frame; shaped reward is its upgrade | SNAKE-01–SNAKE-08, SHAPE-01, SHAPE-02, DATA-01, RUN-09 |
| 3 | `TankEnv` | tank.env | fish, view → Env | one side `fish` of 1 or 5 fish in a radius-10 circle, AD-13's step order (speed cap, wall stop, ball moved with the exact-point mirror bounce, removal within 0.75), seed-set ball direction and fish spots, the 11 view numbers within the short or wide radius, +0.1 survival reward, TANK-10's mean seconds as the score, `all_removed` and `time_cap` endings, ring and circles in the frame | TANK-01–TANK-13, DATA-01, RUN-09 |
| 4 | `HuntEnv` | hunt.env | setup → Env | sides `predator` then `prey` in a radius-10 circle, AD-18's step order (speed caps 3.0 and 2.5, wall stops, catches within 0.75, pellets eaten within 0.5 in prey order, respawn at least 2 from every prey), seed-set prey and pellet spots, the predator's 11 and each prey's 14 view numbers within radius 4, pellet and catch rewards, (catches, pellets per prey) as the scores, `all_caught` and `time_cap` endings, ring, circles, pellets and REPLAY-10's labels in the frame; shares no code with `tank` | HUNT-01–HUNT-15, DATA-01, REPLAY-10 |
| 5 | `run_streams` | train.seeds | seed, Side → RunStreams | AD-05's `SeedSequence` children in order: training game seeds, then per side in `Env.sides` order its move-choice, batch and network-init streams, then opponent draws | RUN-03, RUN-09 |
| 6 | `make_agents` | registry.agents | learner, params, env, setup, RunStreams → Agent | name → constructor dict per environment (Snake's five learners, the tank's four, predator-prey's `pg-ppo` on both sides of a rung or the named side of a control, each environment's `random` and `scripted`, on `setup["side"]` in predator-prey), one entry per side, missing params rejected, `UnknownName` with the environment's own choices | RUN-01, COEV-01, COEV-05 |
| 7 | `RandomPlayer` | baselines.random | rng → Agent | uniform draw from the 3 moves, `scores` None | BASE-01 |
| 8 | `ScriptedBot` | baselines.scripted | obs → Agent | safe move toward food, else closest safe, else straight, read through `Fact` | BASE-02, BASE-03 |
| 9 | `RandomSwimmer` | baselines.swimmer | rng, max_speed → Agent | per row, a direction uniform on the circle and a speed uniform in [0, `max_speed`] (2.5 for any fish or prey, 3.0 for the predator), `scores` None | BASE-04 |
| 10 | `FleeBot` | baselines.flee | obs → Agent | per row, 2.5 straight away from the ball's relative position when `BALL_SEEN`, else (0, 0), read through `View` | BASE-05, BASE-06 |
| 11 | `ChaseBot` | baselines.chase | obs → Agent | 3.0 straight toward the nearest prey's relative position when `PREY_SEEN`, else (0, 0), read through `PredatorView` | BASE-08, BASE-09 |
| 12 | `ForageBot` | baselines.forage | obs → Agent | per row, 2.5 straight away from the predator when `PRED_SEEN`, else 2.5 toward the nearest pellet when `PELLET_SEEN`, else (0, 0), read through `PreyView` | BASE-10–BASE-12 |
| 13 | `TabularQ` | tabular.q | TabularSettings → Learner | dict of 3-score rows keyed by obs, one-step update counted in `updates`, linear `Explore`, `frozen()` as a copied table, `.npz` save and load | TAB-01–TAB-04 |
| 14 | `DQNAgent` | dqn.agent | DQNSettings → Learner | plain rung: 11→64→64→3 MLP, batch of one, hand-written target and squared loss, Adam, `updates` per gradient step, `frozen()` as a copied network, `.pt` save and load; replay, target and double rungs are its upgrades | DQN-01–DQN-04, DQN-06, DQN-07, QR-02 |
| 15 | `PGAgent` | pg.agent | PGSettings → Learner | REINFORCE rung: `n_obs`→64→64→4 MLP as a diagonal Gaussian per row, log spread clamped to [−5, 1], a sample with `rng` in training and the mean in tests, each fish's discounted returns, one update once every agent of its side has ended, hand-written loss computed only through `discounted_returns`, `move_weights` and `gaussian_log_prob`, `last_update` holding each update's `UpdateLog` (AD-15), `updates` counted, `frozen()` copying the policy alone, Adam, `.pt` save and load; one agent per predator-prey side; baseline, actor-critic and PPO rungs (adding `ppo_objective` and `minibatch_plan`) are its upgrades | PG-01–PG-04, PG-06–PG-08, COEV-01, QR-02 |
| 16 | `begin_run` | store.runs | RunSettings → StagedRun | new `<timestamp>-<id>` folder under the environment and setup label (reward, `fish-<n>-<view>`, or the match or baseline side), `settings.json` with library versions, `running` status | RUN-02, RUN-03, RUN-05, SHAPE-03, BASE-07 |
| 17 | `RunFolder` | store.staged | RunSettings, path → StagedRun | `add_point` appends to `curve.json`; `add_version` writes `versions/<side>-<k>.pt`; `finish` writes `agent-<side>` per trained side and `test_games.jsonl` (moves as exact JSON numbers), then `status.json` last, each by temp-file rename | RUN-02, RUN-06, TOUR-01 |
| 18 | `train_loop` | train.loop | Env, Agent, RunStreams, RunSettings, StagedRun, progress → RunRecord | the schedule from `make_schedule`; seeded games (AD-05), each starting with `start_game`; each step every side's lineup agent acts with `explore=True` on its acting rows, each learning side observes once per acting agent in agent order (AD-11), then `current`; at each evenly spaced curve point each learning side tested against its `test_lineup`; with `versions` 10 each learning side saved at every second point; finish complete; a baseline skips the schedule and tests once; 60-minute stop and Ctrl-C are its upgrades | RUN-01, RUN-03, RUN-04, RUN-06–RUN-09, PG-06, COEV-02–COEV-05, TOUR-01, QR-01 |
| 19 | `make_schedule` | registry.schedules | env, setup, Agent, params → Schedule | `solo` for Snake and the tank; predator-prey's match → `together`, `turns`, `pool` or `control` with the other side's scripted bot; intervals from `params` (AD-17); `UnknownName` for an unknown match | COEV-02–COEV-05 |
| 20 | `colearn.make_schedule` | colearn.make | kind, Learner, Agent → Schedule | kind → schedule class, sides and intervals checked, `ValueError` for a kind that doesn't fit | COEV-02–COEV-05 |
| 21 | `SoloSchedule` | colearn.solo | Learner → Schedule | the one side always learning, the same lineup every game, `versions` 0 | RUN-01 |
| 22 | `TogetherSchedule` | colearn.together | Learner → Schedule | both sides' learners, both learning every step, `versions` 10 | COEV-02 |
| 23 | `TurnsSchedule` | colearn.turns | Learner → Schedule | side 0 learns first against the other's frozen copy; straight after the learning side's `swap_every`-th update of its turn it stops, and the other side starts at the next `start_game`; `versions` 10 | COEV-03 |
| 24 | `PoolSchedule` | colearn.pool | Learner → Schedule | turns as `TurnsSchedule`; each side's `Pool` starts with its starting policy and adds `frozen()` every `copy_every` of its updates, keeping `pool_size`; the side not learning is a uniform draw from its pool at each `start_game`; `versions` 10 | COEV-04 |
| 25 | `ControlSchedule` | colearn.control | Learner, Agent → Schedule | the learner's side always learns against the other side's scripted bot, `versions` 0 | COEV-05 |
| 26 | `test_lineup` | registry.lineups | env, side, Agent → Agent | the tested agent on its side, every other side its scripted bot (the chaser for the predator, the forager for the prey); `(agent,)` in a one-side environment | EVAL-01, BASE-07 |
| 27 | `test_agent` | evaluate.test | Env, Agent → TestResult | test seeds 0–99 through `play_game` with the side's lineup, mean of the tested side's game `score` | EVAL-01, EVAL-02, SHAPE-04 |
| 28 | `play_game` | evaluate.play | Env, Agent, seed, rng → TestGame | greedy moves on every side's acting rows until done, recording each step's moves, scores and game scores per side | TAB-03, DQN-06, PG-07, HUNT-15 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def train_run(settings: RunSettings, *, root: Path = Path("runs"),
              progress: Callable[[CurvePoint], None] | None = None) -> RunRecord:
    env = make_env(settings.env, settings.setup)
    streams = run_streams(settings.seed, env.sides)
    agents = make_agents(settings.learner, settings.params, env=settings.env,
                         setup=settings.setup, init_seeds=streams.init_seeds)
    staged = begin_run(settings, root=root)
    return train_loop(env, agents, streams, settings, staged, progress)
```

## PL-02: Compare and report
**Entry:** `src/rllab/compare/pipeline.py::build_report` · **Trigger:** `rllab compare`
**Purpose:** Turn the complete runs and tournaments into one report per environment, each with its baseline and ladder comparisons, learning-curve and tournament charts, trend tests, and whether that environment is done.
**Covers:** EVAL-03–EVAL-08, BASE-07, DQN-05, SHAPE-05, PG-05, COEV-06, TOUR-03, TOUR-04, RUN-06, RUN-09

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `list_runs` | store.listing | runs root, complete_only → RunRecord | scan the folder tree, read settings, status and curve; `running` reads as incomplete | RUN-06 |
| 2 | `compare_baselines` | compare.baselines | RunRecord → Comparison, waiting | each environment's learners and setups with 5 complete seeds, each learning side against both of that side's baselines run on the same setup (predator-prey: recorded on that side), same 100 test seeds, test and correction set up with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill within that environment's report (AD-08, AD-14); short groups go to waiting | EVAL-04, BASE-07 |
| 3 | `load_test_games` | store.games | RunRecord → TestGame | read `test_games.jsonl` in side order, then seed order | EVAL-04 |
| 4 | `compare_ladder` | compare.ladder | RunRecord, Comparison, waiting → Comparison, waiting | each environment's EVAL-05 pairs from `build_report`'s table, per side in predator-prey, the same way; a control step carries EVAL-08's note; a pair whose saved settings differ beyond the added piece goes to waiting (AD-03, AD-12, AD-17) | EVAL-05, EVAL-08, DQN-05, SHAPE-05, PG-05, COEV-06 |
| 5 | `load_grids` | compare.grids | RunRecord, tournaments root → TournamentGrid | each complete ladder run's grid through `load_tournament`; a run with none is left out | TOUR-03 |
| 6 | `load_tournament` | store.tournaments | RunRecord → TournamentGrid | PL-04 as built | TOUR-03 |
| 7 | `test_trends` | compare.trends | RunRecord, TournamentGrid, Comparison, waiting → Comparison, waiting | per rung with 5 seed runs' grids, the two trend tests (catches rising with the predator's version, falling with the prey's) through the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill on each seed run's 100 pairing scores, within the report's correction; a trend only when both are significant, `p_adjusted` the larger (AD-19); a rung short of grids goes to waiting, naming the missing seeds | TOUR-03, TOUR-04 |
| 8 | `read_explanations` | compare.explain | Comparison, reports dir → Comparison | read `<env>/explanations/<comparison id>.md` for every comparison and tournament; empty or missing is unexplained | EVAL-06, TOUR-04 |
| 9 | `draw_curves` | compare.charts | RunRecord, reports dir → Chart | one PNG per learner, setup and learning side under `reports/<env>/`: seed mean line, seed range band, the same setup and side's baselines as lines, `dataviz` palette (AD-09) | EVAL-03, BASE-07 |
| 10 | `draw_heatmaps` | compare.heatmaps | TournamentGrid, Chart, reports dir → Chart | per rung with 5 grids, one 10 × 10 heatmap PNG of the cell means (predator version by prey version) under `reports/hunt/`, `dataviz` palette (AD-09), added to the curve charts | TOUR-03 |
| 11 | `write_report` | compare.report | Comparison, waiting, Chart, reports dir → Report | one `reports/<env>/report.md` per environment with runs: comparison and trend table with notes, waiting list, chart links, and the EVAL-07 done check for that environment's top learners (predator-prey: both sides of `rung-3` plus every tournament) with each gap named (AD-14) | EVAL-03–EVAL-08, TOUR-03, RUN-09 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def build_report(*, runs: Path = Path("runs"), tournaments: Path = Path("tournaments"),
                 out: Path = Path("reports")) -> tuple[Report, ...]:
    records = list_runs(root=runs, complete_only=True)
    comparisons, waiting = compare_baselines(records)
    comparisons, waiting = compare_ladder(records, comparisons, waiting)
    grids = load_grids(records, tournaments)
    comparisons, waiting = test_trends(records, grids, comparisons, waiting)
    comparisons = read_explanations(comparisons, out)
    charts = draw_curves(records, out)
    charts = draw_heatmaps(grids, charts, out)
    return write_report(comparisons, waiting, charts, out)
```

## PL-03: Watch a replay
**Entry:** `src/rllab/replay/pipeline.py::run_replay` · **Trigger:** `rllab replay RUN_ID [--game I | --seed N] [--side S] [--speed V]`
**Purpose:** Watch any saved test game of a run, or its agent playing a fresh seed, in a window showing Snake's move scores or each learning fish's swim arrow and spread, with pause, step and speed.
**Covers:** REPLAY-01–REPLAY-10, RUN-08, RUN-09, QR-02

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `list_runs` | store.listing | runs root → RunRecord | PL-02 as built | REPLAY-01 |
| 2 | `find_run` | replay.find | RunRecord, run id → RunRecord | exact `run_id` match; `FileNotFoundError` naming a missing run | REPLAY-01 |
| 3 | `open_game` | replay.open | RunRecord, index, seed, side → ReplayGame | saved game `index` of `side`'s tested games (neither `index` nor `seed`: game 0; no `side`: the run's first learning or recorded side) through `load_test_games`, `make_env` and `rebuild_game`; a fresh `seed` through `load_agent`, `test_lineup` and `play_game` is its upgrade | REPLAY-01, REPLAY-07, RUN-09 |
| 4 | `load_test_games` | store.games | RunRecord → TestGame | PL-02 as built | REPLAY-01 |
| 5 | `make_env` | registry.envs | env, setup → Env | PL-01 as built | RUN-08 |
| 6 | `rebuild_game` | replay.rebuild | Env, TestGame → ReplayGame | reset the env to the game's seed, step its moves, collect frames, `ReplayMismatch` at the first differing game score or ending | REPLAY-06, RUN-09 |
| 7 | `load_agent` | registry.load | RunRecord, side → Agent | a baseline rebuilt by environment, side and name; a learner from its `agent-<side>` file | REPLAY-07 |
| 8 | `test_lineup` | registry.lineups | env, side, Agent → Agent | PL-01 as built | REPLAY-07 |
| 9 | `play_game` | evaluate.play | Env, Agent, seed, rng → TestGame | PL-01 as built | REPLAY-07 |
| 10 | `show` | replay.window | ReplayGame, speed → ShowLog | pygame-ce window drawing each `Frame`'s cells, circles and rings scaled from its bounds, with its labels, the step number and the ending, at the set speed; per side Snake's score bars, each fish's arrows and spreads, "n/a" for a baseline side, and the pause, step and speed keys are its upgrades | REPLAY-02–REPLAY-05, REPLAY-08–REPLAY-10, QR-02 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def run_replay(run_id: str, *, index: int | None = None, seed: int | None = None,
               side: str | None = None, speed: int = 10, runs: Path = Path("runs")) -> ShowLog:
    records = list_runs(root=runs)
    record = find_run(records, run_id)
    game = open_game(record, index=index, seed=seed, side=side)
    return show(game, speed=speed)
```

Preview (first 2KB):
### core.Side: type
`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Side: name: str; n_agents: int; obs_size: int; actions: ActionSpace`
- **Invariants:** an environment's sides have a fixed order and unique names (AD-16). Snake: `snake` (1 agent, 11 obs, discrete). The tank: `fish` (1 or 5, 11 obs, capped at 2.5). Predator-prey: `predator` (1, 11 obs, capped at 3.0), then `prey` (5, 14 obs, capped at 2.5)
- **Boundary:** snake, tank, hunt, registry, evaluate, train, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Side/ · **Cases:** Snake's side; a 5-fish tank side; predator-prey's two sides
- **Used by:** snake, tank, hunt, registry, evaluate, train, replay; PL-01

### core.Frame: type
`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Frame: bounds: tuple[float, float, float, float]; y_down: bool; shapes: tuple[Shape, ...]; labels: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]`
- **Invariants:** `bounds` is `(x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max)` and every shape lies inside it; `y_down` when y grows downward (Snake: `(0, 0, 10, 10)`, true; the tank and predator-prey: `(-10, -10, 10, 10)`, false); shapes draw in order; `labels` are name/value lines (Snake: food eaten; the tank: time in seconds, fish swimming, each removed fish's removal time; predator-prey: time in seconds, pellets eaten, prey caught, each caught prey's capture time) (REPLAY-02, REPLAY-08, REPLAY-10)
- **Boundary:** snake, tank, hunt, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Frame/ · **Cases:** a Snake start frame; a Snake frame at length 5; a 5-fish tank frame after one removal; a predator-prey frame after one catch; a test-env frame
- **Used by:** snake, tank, hunt, replay

### core.Env: type

8	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Frame: bounds: tuple[float, float, float, float]; y_down: bool; shapes: tuple[Shape, ...]; labels: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]`
9	- **Invariants:** `bounds` is `(x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max)` and every shape lies inside it; `y_down` when y grows downward (Snake: `(0, 0, 10, 10)`, true; the tank and predator-prey: `(-10, -10, 10, 10)`, false); shapes draw in order; `labels` are name/value lines (Snake: food eaten; the tank: time in seconds, fish swimming, each removed fish's removal time; predator-prey: time in seconds, pellets eaten, prey caught, each caught prey's capture time) (REPLAY-02, REPLAY-08, REPLAY-10)

13	### core.Env: type
14	`class Env(Protocol): name: str; sides: tuple[Side, ...]; def reset(self, seed: int) -> tuple[np.ndarray, ...]; def step(self, moves: tuple[np.ndarray, ...]) -> Step; def frame(self) -> Frame`
15	- **Invariants:** `reset` returns float32 `(n_agents, obs_size)` per side, every agent acting; `moves[s]` is int64 `(n_agents,)` for discrete moves or float32 `(n_agents, size)` for continuous ones, and rows of agents not acting are ignored; same seed and moves → identical `Step`s and `Frame`s (AD-11, AD-16)
16	- **Errors:** `ValueError` for a discrete move outside `[0, size)`, a non-finite continuous move, or the wrong number of sides or shape; `RuntimeError` for `step` before `reset` or after `done`
17	- **Boundary:** registry, evaluate, train, tournament, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Env/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a recorded 20-move Snake game from seed 7; one of a recorded 30-step 5-fish tank game from seed 7; one of a recorded 30-step predator-prey game from seed 7
18	- **Used by:** registry, evaluate, train, tournament, replay; PL-01, PL-03, PL-04, PL-05

20	### core.Agent: type
21	`class Agent(Protocol): name: str; def act(self, obs: np.ndarray, *, explore: bool, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray; def scores(self, obs: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray | None`
22	- **Invariants:** plays one side: `obs` is that side's rows, and it returns one move per row, each row decided on its own (PG-06), shaped as that side's `Env.step` entry; with `explore=False` a value learner picks its highest score, ties by move order (straight, left, right), and a policy learner its most likely move; `scores` is `(rows, len(score_names))`, or `None` for baselines
23	- **Boundary:** registry, evaluate, train, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Agent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of the scripted bot over a recorded 20-move game; one of a table learner with scores; one of a PPO policy over a recorded 5-fish game; one of the scripted chaser over a recorded predator-prey game
24	- **Used by:** registry, colearn, evaluate, train, replay; PL-01, PL-03, PL-04, PL-05
25	
26	### core.Learner: type
27	`class Learner(Agent, Protocol): updates: int; def observe(self, t: Transition, step: int, rng: np.random.Generator) -> None; def frozen(self) -> Agent; def settings(self) -> dict[str, int | float | str]; def save(self, path: Path) -> None; @classmethod def load(cls, path: Path) -> Learner`
28	- **Invariants:** `observe` is the only place learning happens, called once per acting agent of its side per step, in agent order; `updates` counts the learning updates applied so far, from 0 (the table learner and plain DQN: one per `observe`); `act(explore=True)` is the training behaviour (value learners: `Explore.rate(step)`; policy learners: a sample from the policy); `frozen()` returns a copy that acts as the learner does now and never changes (AD-17); a loaded learner acts exactly like the saved one
29	- **Errors:** `load` raises `FileNotFoundError` or `ValueError` for a missing or unreadable file
30	- **Boundary:** registry, colearn, train, store · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Learner/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a table learner over 50 recorded transitions; one of a plain DQN rung; one of a REINFORCE rung over a recorded 1-fish game; a PPO rung's frozen copy after 2 updates
31	- **Used by:** registry, colearn, train, store; PL-01
32	
33	### core.Lineup: type
34	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Lineup: agents: tuple[Agent, ...]; learns: tuple[bool, ...]`
35	- **Invariants:** one entry per side, in `Env.sides` order; `agents[s]` plays side `s`; where `learns[s]`, `agents[s]` is that side's `Learner`, and only those sides observe (AD-17)
36	- **Boundary:** colearn, train · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Lineup/ · **Cases:** Snake's learner alone; rung 2 with the predator learning against the prey's own frozen learner; rung 3 with the prey learning against a pool copy; the prey control against the scripted chaser
37	- **Used by:** colearn, train; PL-01
38	
39	### core.Schedule: type
40	`class Schedule(Protocol): learners: tuple[Learner | None, ...]; versions: int; def start_game(self, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Lineup; def current(self) -> Lineup`
41	- **Invariants:** `learners[s]` is side `s`'s learner, or `None` for a side the run doesn't train (the others are the run's learning sides); `versions` is 10 for a predator-prey ladder rung, else 0 (TOUR-01); `start_game` runs at each training game's start and draws any opponent with `rng` (COEV-04); `current` runs after every step and reflects any switch due after that step's updates (COEV-03); same calls and learner updates → same lineups
42	- **Boundary:** registry, train · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Schedule/ · **Cases:** a replay double of `solo` over 3 games; one of `turns` across its first swap; one of `pool` over 12 recorded draws
43	- **Used by:** registry, train; PL-01

45	### core.Explore: type
46	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Explore: start: float; floor: float; decay_steps: int` with `def rate(self, step: int) -> float`
47	- **Invariants:** falls linearly from `start` at step 0 to `floor` at `decay_steps`, then stays; `1 ≥ start ≥ floor ≥ 0` (TAB-02, DQN-06)
48	- **Boundary:** tabular, dqn · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Explore/ · **Cases:** 1.0 → 0.05 over 100,000 steps, read at 0, 50,000 and 200,000

115	### colearn.Pool: type
116	`class Pool: def __init__(self, start: Agent, size: int = 10) -> None; copies: tuple[Agent, ...]; def add(self, agent: Agent) -> None; def draw(self, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Agent`
117	- **Invariants:** starts holding `start` alone; the first `add` replaces it, and from then on it keeps the latest `size` copies, oldest first; `draw` picks uniformly from `copies`, the latest included (COEV-04 and its edge case)
118	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `size` is below 1
119	- **Boundary:** tests · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/colearn.Pool/ · **Cases:** a fresh pool; 11 copies added; 10,000 draws from 10 copies with rng seed 3
120	- **Used by:** colearn
121	
122	### colearn.make_schedule: Build a schedule
123	`def make_schedule(kind: Literal["solo", "together", "turns", "pool", "control"], learners: tuple[Learner | None, ...], bots: tuple[Agent | None, ...], *, swap_every: int = 20, copy_every: int = 20, pool_size: int = 10) -> Schedule`
124	- **Invariants:** one entry per side in `learners` and `bots`, each side having exactly one; `solo`: one side, always learning; `together`: both learn every step (COEV-02); `turns`: side 0 first, a side stops learning straight after its `swap_every`-th update of the turn, and the other starts at the next `start_game` (COEV-03); `pool`: as `turns`, the side not learning played by a draw from its `Pool` at each `start_game`, each side adding `frozen()` every `copy_every` of its updates (COEV-04); `control`: the learner's side always learns, against the other side's bot (COEV-05); `versions` 10 for `together`, `turns`, `pool`, else 0 (AD-17)
125	- **Errors:** `ValueError` for a kind that doesn't fit the given sides, or an interval below 1
126	- **Boundary:** registry · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/colearn.make_schedule/ · **Cases:** `solo` for Snake; `together`; `turns` over 45 recorded predator updates; `pool` over 12 games; the predator `control`
127	- **Used by:** registry; PL-01

132	- **Invariants:** one entry per side: the agent for each side the run trains or records, `None` for a side played only by its opponents; Snake's learners are `tabular`, `dqn-plain`, `dqn-replay`, `dqn-target`, `dqn-double`; the tank's are `pg-reinforce`, `pg-baseline`, `pg-actor-critic`, `pg-ppo`; predator-prey's is `pg-ppo`, on both sides for a rung and the named side for a control (COEV-01, COEV-05); `random` and `scripted` build the environment's baselines, on `setup["side"]` in predator-prey; `params` holds every `default_params` key (AD-10); learners are `Learner`s seeded from their side's `init_seeds` entry

204	### store.StagedRun: type
205	`class StagedRun(Protocol): run_id: str; path: Path; def add_point(self, point: CurvePoint) -> None; def add_version(self, side: str, k: int, learner: Learner) -> None; def finish(self, status: Literal["complete", "incomplete", "over_time"], *, agents: Mapping[str, Agent], games: Sequence[TestGame]) -> RunRecord`
206	- **Invariants:** `add_point` writes at once; `add_version` saves `versions/<side>-<k>.pt`, `k` 1–10 (TOUR-01); `finish` writes each trained side's agent and the games (moves as exact JSON numbers, AD-06), then `status.json` last; every file written by rename; a failed write gives `incomplete` with `missing` set

208	- **Used by:** train; PL-01

250	## train
251	
252	### train.RunStreams: type
253	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class RunStreams: game_seeds: np.random.Generator; moves: tuple[np.random.Generator, ...]; batches: tuple[np.random.Generator, ...]; init_seeds: tuple[int, ...]; opponents: np.random.Generator`
254	- **Invariants:** built from `np.random.SeedSequence(seed)`'s children in AD-05's order: `game_seeds` first, then for each side in `Env.sides` order its `moves`, `batches` and `init_seeds` entries, then `opponents`; each per-side tuple has one entry per side; `game_seeds` yields training game seeds in `[1_000, 2^31)`, never a test seed; `moves[s]` goes to side `s`'s `act`, `batches[s]` to its `observe`, `init_seeds[s]` to `registry.make_agents`, `opponents` to `Schedule.start_game`; same seed and sides → same streams, and a one-side run's streams don't depend on any other environment (RUN-03, RUN-09)
255	- **Boundary:** registry, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/train.RunStreams/ · **Cases:** Snake's streams from seed 0, with its first 3 game seeds; predator-prey's two-side streams from seed 0; seed 0's `snake` streams equal to the `fish` streams of a 1-fish tank from seed 0
256	- **Used by:** train, registry, replay; PL-01, PL-05

258	### train.train_run: One training run
259	`def train_run(settings: RunSettings, *, root: Path = Path("runs"), progress: Callable[[CurvePoint], None] | None = None) -> RunRecord`
260	- **Invariants:** one environment setup, one seed, the agents of `registry.make_agents` and the schedule of `registry.make_schedule` (RUN-01, AD-17); each training game starts with `start_game`; each step, every side's lineup agent acts on its acting rows with `explore=True`, each learning side observes once per acting agent in agent order (PG-06, AD-11), then `current`; `curve_points` evenly spaced test rounds, each learning side against `registry.test_lineup`, each point passed to `progress` (RUN-04)
261	- **Invariants (run):** with `versions` 10, each learning side is saved at every second point (TOUR-01); a baseline plays its test lineup once; stops at `max_minutes` as `over_time` (RUN-07); Ctrl-C ends `incomplete` (RUN-06); same settings → same curve (RUN-03)
262	- **Errors:** `UnknownName` before any folder exists; a save failure returns an `incomplete` record
263	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/train.train_run/ · **Cases:** a recorded 3-point tabular run; an over-time run; a recorded 3-point 5-fish PPO run; a recorded 4-point rung-2 run with 2 versions per side
264	- **Used by:** cli; PL-01

303	`def build_report(*, runs: Path = Path("runs"), tournaments: Path = Path("tournaments"), out: Path = Path("reports")) -> tuple[Report, ...]`
304	- **Invariants:** one report per environment with runs (AD-14), every test through the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill on complete runs only, corrected within that report (AD-08); EVAL-04 for each learner setup's learning side against that side's baselines of the same setup (BASE-07); a pair whose settings differ beyond the added piece is refused (DQN-05, SHAPE-05, PG-05, COEV-06); charts per the `dataviz` skill (EVAL-03, TOUR-03)

307	- **Predator-prey (28 tests):** EVAL-04 for each side of `control-<side>`, `rung-1`, `rung-2`, `rung-3` against that side's `random` and `scripted`; EVAL-05 per side `control-<side>`→`rung-1`→`rung-2`→`rung-3`, the control step with EVAL-08's note; two trend tests per rung (TOUR-04); top learners both sides of `rung-3`, plus every tournament (EVAL-07)
308	- **Errors:** a refused or short pair, or a rung missing grids, goes to `waiting`, never raised
309	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/compare.build_report/ · **Cases:** 5 seeds of two Snake rungs and both baselines; 5 seeds of two tank rungs and both tank baselines; 5 seeds of `rung-1` and `rung-2` with both sides' baselines and their 10 grids

340	### replay.open_game: Rebuild a game to watch
341	`def open_game(record: RunRecord, *, index: int | None = None, seed: int | None = None, side: str | None = None) -> ReplayGame`
342	- **Invariants:** at most one of `index` (a saved test game, rebuilt from its seed and moves, REPLAY-01, REPLAY-06) or `seed` (the agent plays greedily on a fresh seed of the run's setup in its side's test lineup, REPLAY-07); neither means saved game 0; `side` picks which tested side's games `index` counts and which side plays a fresh seed, by default the run's first learning or recorded side
343	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if both `index` and `seed` are given, or for a side the run didn't test; `FileNotFoundError` or `IndexError` naming the missing item; `class ReplayMismatch(RuntimeError): move: int; expected: str; got: str`, at the first step whose game score or ending differs

348	`def open_match(env: str, picks: Mapping[str, RunRecord | None], *, seed: int) -> ReplayGame`
349	- **Invariants:** one pick per side; a record plays its saved agent for that side, `None` that side's scripted bot; played greedily from `seed`; same picks and seed → same game (REPLAY-11)
350	- **Errors:** `ValueError` for a side missing from `picks` or a record with no agent for that side; `FileNotFoundError` naming a missing agent file
351	- **Boundary:** replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/replay.open_match/ · **Cases:** two runs' predator and prey on seed 12345, played twice; a saved predator against the scripted forager
352	- **Used by:** replay; PL-05
353	
354	### replay.show: Watch a replay
355	`def show(game: ReplayGame, *, speed: int = 10) -> ShowLog`
356	- **Invariants:** a pygame-ce window drawing each frame's shapes and labels, the step number and the ending (REPLAY-02, REPLAY-08, REPLAY-10); per side, by its `actions`: discrete moves get one bar per `score_names` entry with the chosen move marked, continuous moves an arrow from each acting agent's shape along its move with its spread (REPLAY-03, REPLAY-09); "n/a" for a side whose `scores` are `None`; space pauses and resumes, → steps while paused, ↑/↓ set 1–30 steps per second (REPLAY-04, REPLAY-05); returns what was shown when the window closes
357	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `speed` is outside 1–30
358	- **Boundary:** replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/replay.show/ · **Cases:** a recorded event script (pause, step, speed up) on a 12-move game; the same script on a 5-fish game; on a predator-prey game with a scripted side
359	- **Used by:** replay; PL-03, PL-05

363	### cli.main: The rllab command
364	`def main(argv: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> int`
365	- **Invariants:** `rllab train [--env snake|tank|hunt] --learner L [--reward R | --fish F --view V | --match M | --side S] --seed N [--steps S]` (`--env` defaults to `snake`; a baseline name records it); `rllab tournament`; `rllab compare` (every environment's report); `rllab replay RUN_ID [--game I | --seed N] [--side S] [--speed V]` calls `replay.run_replay`; `rllab replay --env hunt --predator RUN_ID|scripted --prey RUN_ID|scripted --seed N [--speed V]` calls `replay.run_match`; `train` merges the user's overrides into `registry.default_params` before `train.train_run` (AD-10); one progress line per curve point, every learning side's score on it; 0 on success

6:`rllab` is one Python package, run from the command line. It holds three environments, Snake, the fish tank and predator-prey, on one shared core. Every environment is one or more sides of agents stepped together: Snake's one snake, the tank's school of fish, and predator-prey's predator and five prey. A training run builds one environment setup, an agent for each side it trains, and a schedule. The schedule decides, game by game and step by step, which agent plays each side and which sides learn: Snake and the tank always have their one side learning, while predator-prey's ladder rungs and controls follow the co-learning rules. At each of 20 curve points the run pauses training and plays 100 reserved test games greedily for each side that learns, each against its side's fixed test opponents, then writes the point to the run's folder. A predator-prey ladder run also saves 10 versions of each side as it goes. When the run finishes it saves its agents and test games, and writes the run's status last. A separate tournament command plays every saved predator version against every saved prey version. Comparisons read only complete runs and their tournaments, write one report per environment, and hand the per-game scores to the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill. The replay viewer rebuilds any saved game, or a fresh pairing of saved agents, by stepping the environment through its moves. It draws Snake's move scores, or each learning fish's swim arrow and spread. Training, testing, storage, the tournament, comparison and replay see only the `core` protocols and the registry, never an environment, a learner or a schedule (AD-04, AD-11, AD-16).
26:| Numerics and tank simulations | NumPy ≥ 2.0 | AD-05, AD-13, AD-18 |
27:| Run storage | Folder per run: JSON, NumPy `.npz`, PyTorch `.pt`, JSON Lines; tournament grids as JSON | AD-06, AD-19 |
69:- **Experience (DATA-01):** every game is an environment from `registry.make_env`, started from a seed. In each environment, seeds 0–99 are the test seeds (`evaluate.test_agent`), and the tournament plays seeds 0–19 of them (AD-19). Training game seeds are drawn from the run seed's generator in `[1_000, 2^31)`, so they never meet a test seed (AD-05).
82:- **Tournaments (TOUR-02):** `tournaments/hunt/pg-ppo/<rung>/seed-<n>/<run id>.json` holds one ladder run's 10 × 10 grid, written once and never overwritten (AD-19).
87:- **Reproducibility (RUN-03, RUN-09, SNAKE-08, TANK-13, HUNT-15):** every random draw comes from a `np.random.Generator` or `torch.Generator` derived from the run seed. PyTorch runs on the CPU with one thread and deterministic algorithms (AD-05). Adding an environment changes no code path, seed stream, setup label or file format of the earlier ones, so their saved runs rerun, compare (AD-14) and replay identically. `hunt` shares no simulation code with `tank` (AD-18).
88:- **Time (QR-01, RUN-07):** `train` checks the wall clock after every step and at every test game. At 60 minutes it stops and saves what it has. Elapsed seconds are saved with each curve point. A step is one environment step, whatever the number of sides or agents (AD-11, AD-16). Tournament games run outside the run, under their own command (AD-19).
126:- **Decision:** Environments implement `core.Env`; agents implement `core.Agent`, and learners `core.Learner`. The runners (`train`, `evaluate`, `store`, `tournament`, `compare`, `replay`) import only `core`, `registry` and each other, never an environment, a learner or a schedule; those come from `registry`. `core` holds only what the lab's environments use; AD-11 and AD-16 set its shape. A test-only grid walk proves RUN-08.
134:  - **Streams:** a run seed builds a `np.random.SeedSequence`. Its first child gives training game seeds. Each side then gets three children of its own: move choice (exploration or sampling from the policy), batch sampling, and network initialisation (a `torch.Generator`). One last child gives opponent draws (AD-17). Children are spawned in that order, side by side in `Env.sides` order, so a one-side run's streams don't depend on what other environments use. `train.RunStreams` carries them from the run seed to `registry.make_agents` (each side's `init_seeds` entry) and through the training loop.
187:- **Context:** RUN-03 reruns a run from its saved settings. AD-03, AD-12 and AD-17 refuse a ladder pair whose saved settings differ beyond the added piece (DQN-05, SHAPE-05, PG-05, COEV-06). Pipeline design found that nothing filled a learner's defaults before `store.begin_run` writes `settings.json`, so a run using defaults would save an incomplete `params`. UR-37: "Go ahead with the five contract changes."
<!-- docs/architecture.md:280 -->
### AD-17: Co-learning is a schedule the training loop follows
**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** colearn
- **Context:** UR-80 picked the ladder "1 both learn at once -> 2 take turns -> 3 train against a pool of the opponent's past versions", with the one-side control (COEV-02–COEV-05). UR-86: "Run the control in both directions, 25 runs." COEV-06 requires each setup to differ from the one it is compared with only by the added piece. RUN-01 trains one rung or control per run. TOUR-01 saves 10 versions of each side per ladder run. AD-04 keeps `train` from knowing any environment or learner.
- **Decision:**
  - **The protocol:** `core.Schedule` is what `train` follows. At each training game's start, `start_game` gives a `core.Lineup`: the agent playing each side, and whether that side learns. After every step, `current` gives the lineup again. `train` acts with every side's agent and calls `observe` only for the sides that learn.
  - **The schedules:** `colearn` implements one schedule per match, and `registry` builds it from the run's setup.
    - **`solo`:** the only side always learns (Snake and the tank).
    - **`together`:** rung 1, both sides learn every step.
    - **`turns`:** rung 2. The predator learns first, and a side stops learning straight after its 20th update of the turn. The other side starts at the next game.
    - **`pool`:** rung 3. Turns as in rung 2. The learning side faces a copy drawn at the game's start, uniformly from the opponent's pool of its last 10 copies. Each side adds a `Learner.frozen()` copy at every 20th update; a pool with no copies yet holds the side's starting policy.
    - **`control`:** the other side is played by its scripted bot, and every other setting is rung 1's.
  - **What the schedules read:** `Learner.updates` counts each learner's updates.
  - **Settings:** the swap interval, copy interval and pool size are saved in `params` as `colearn.swap_every`, `colearn.copy_every` and `colearn.pool_size`, set only on the rungs that use them.
  - **Acting in training:** every side acts with `explore=True`, frozen sides and pool copies included, so a side faces the opponent as it trained.
  - **Versions:** a ladder schedule asks for 10 versions, and `train` saves each learning side at every second curve point, the last at the end.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Rung logic inside `train`: the loop would know predator-prey's sides, against AD-04.
  - A separate co-learning training loop: copies the loop, its curve points and its time limit, against UR-53.
  - Swapping turns mid-game: the newly learning side would start partway through a game, and in rung 3 it would face an opponent it didn't draw.
  - Frozen sides acting greedily: the learning side would train against a sharper opponent than the one its partner trained as.
- **Consequences:** Snake and the tank run through the same loop with `solo`. The steps between a side's 20th update and the end of that game train no one, a few hundred steps per turn. Pool copies stay in memory: 10 small networks per side. A new rung (league play, say) adds a schedule.
- **Probe:** none — no external API.
<!-- docs/architecture.md:303 -->
### AD-18: Predator-prey simulated in its own NumPy environment, a fixed 0.1-second step
**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** hunt
- **Context:** HUNT-03 caps the predator at 3.0 and each prey at 2.5 units per second; HUNT-04 stops both at the wall. HUNT-06 settles catches at the end of each step, and HUNT-07 settles pellets after catches. The edge cases give ties to the lower-numbered prey or pellet. HUNT-15 needs identical games from a seed. RUN-09 needs saved tank games to replay unchanged, and AD-13 makes the tank's step order part of its games. UR-81: "same tank, moves and PPO, learning predator replaces the ball".
- **Decision:** `hunt.HuntEnv` holds positions as NumPy arrays and runs each step in a fixed order:
  1. Cap each move's speed.
  2. Move the predator and every prey still swimming, stopping each at the wall (centre at most 9.5 from the centre for the predator, 9.75 for a prey).
  3. Catch every prey within 0.75 of the predator.
  4. In prey order, let each prey still swimming eat every pellet within 0.5.
  5. Respawn each eaten pellet, in pellet order, by drawing uniform spots within 9.5 of the centre from the game's generator until one lies at least 2 units from every prey still swimming.

  Views are computed for all fish at once. A relative velocity is taken over the step just run, and is (0, 0) at reset, as in the tank. `hunt` shares no code with `tank`.
- **Alternatives:** Reuse `tank`'s simulation with the ball swapped for a predator: `tank` would change for predator-prey's sake, and any slip would change saved tank games (RUN-09). Shared geometry helpers in `core`: the same risk, and `core` would hold simulation code. Pellets respawning anywhere: a pellet could appear under a prey and be eaten in the same step, against HUNT-07.
- **Consequences:** About 150 lines are close to `tank`'s, a cost accepted so that tank games can't shift. One step is a few dozen array operations, as in the tank. The step order is part of the game.
- **Probe:** none — no external API.
<!-- docs/architecture.md:318 -->
### AD-19: The tournament is its own command, its grids stored beside the runs
**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** tournament
- **Context:** TOUR-02 plays all 100 version pairings of each ladder run on 20 held-out tanks. QR-01 keeps tournament games out of the run's hour. TOUR-03 charts each rung from its 5 seed runs, and TOUR-04 tests each rung for a trend. AD-06 keeps a run folder unchanged once its status is written.
- **Decision:**
  - **The command:** `rllab tournament` (`tournament.run_tournaments`) finds every complete ladder run with no grid. For each one, it plays every predator version against every prey version, both greedy, on test seeds 0–19. It saves the mean catches per game of each pairing as a 10 × 10 grid.
  - **Where grids go:** each grid is saved once with `store.save_tournament`, under `tournaments/`. A grid already there is never replayed or overwritten.
  - **Comparison:** `compare` reads the grids, draws each rung's chart, and runs each rung's two trend tests (rising with the predator's version, falling with the prey's). Together they count as one result, which shows a trend only when both are significant.
- **Alternatives:** Play the tournament at the end of each run: it would count against the hour (QR-01), and a crash in it would cost the run. Play it inside `compare`: `compare` would depend on the registry and play games on every rebuild. Write the grid into the run folder: the folder would change after its status, against AD-06.
- **Consequences:** A rung's chart waits until all 5 of its runs have grids, and the report lists the runs still waiting. The grids hold 12,000 games per rung (Risks).
- **Probe:** none — no external API.
<!-- docs/architecture.md:130 -->
### AD-05: Seeds, test-seed reservation and CPU determinism
**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system
- **Context:** RUN-03 needs identical curves on a rerun. SNAKE-08, TANK-13, HUNT-15, TAB-03, DQN-06, PG-07 and REPLAY-07 need repeatable games. EVAL-01 needs test seeds never used in training. RUN-09 needs each environment's draws untouched by the ones added after it. COEV-04 draws opponents at random.
- **Decision:**
  - **Streams:** a run seed builds a `np.random.SeedSequence`. Its first child gives training game seeds. Each side then gets three children of its own: move choice (exploration or sampling from the policy), batch sampling, and network initialisation (a `torch.Generator`). One last child gives opponent draws (AD-17). Children are spawned in that order, side by side in `Env.sides` order, so a one-side run's streams don't depend on what other environments use. `train.RunStreams` carries them from the run seed to `registry.make_agents` (each side's `init_seeds` entry) and through the training loop.
  - **Seed ranges:** test seeds are 0–99; training game seeds are drawn from `[1_000, 2^31)`.
  - **Torch:** runs with `torch.set_num_threads(1)` and `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True)`.
  - **Per game:** each game owns its own generator from its game seed, so food placement, the ball's start direction, every fish's and pellet's start spot and every pellet's respawn depend only on the seed and the moves.
- **Alternatives:** A global seed (`np.random.seed`): any new random call shifts every later draw. One shared move-choice stream for both sides: a change to one side's acting would shift the other's draws. Several threads: faster on big networks, but the reduction order varies between runs.
- **Consequences:** Results reproduce on the same laptop and library versions, not across machines; `settings.json` records the versions. One thread stays fast enough for these networks (Risks).

# The user's words: `docs/requests.md`

Every planning skill follows this. The brief and the spec compress, and the next skill often runs in a fresh session that sees only the docs. `docs/requests.md` keeps what the user actually said, word for word, so the skill whose doc must honour it can. `dt request` writes every line; never edit the file by hand.

## What goes in

One request (`UR-NN`) per concrete statement you could check a doc against:

- facts about the domain, the data or the world ("the tank is circular"), numbers, limits, deadlines, budgets
- tools, libraries, vendors, protocols and data sources the user names ("stream prices over a WebSocket", "use yahooquery")
- choices: an option picked from yours ("Go with A"), and the answer to any question a skill asked
- a block acceptance ("assumptions 1–7 are fine"): one request, its `--about` listing what was accepted
- scope: what must be in, or left out

Not: approval of a whole doc ("approved", "looks good"), wishes with nothing to check ("make it nice"), your own proposals the user hasn't picked, or words already recorded (`dt request` lists them).

## Recording

As you hear it, before you write any doc:

### TC-38: Predator-prey rules hold on every step of three kinds of play
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** HUNT-01–HUNT-11, DATA-01 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-38/
**Source:** the spec's rules (HUNT-01–HUNT-11 and the HUNT edge cases) and AD-18's step order; generator `hunt_games(game_seeds=1000–1049, rng_seed=3)` playing three lineups: `RandomSwimmer(3.0)` against `RandomSwimmer(2.5)` prey; `ChaseBot` against `ForageBot` prey; `ChaseBot` against a test-only `grazer` whose prey swim at 2.5 to the nearest seen pellet and ignore the predator (so catches beside pellets and shared pellets happen); positions read from each `Frame`'s ring and circles
**Expected:** at `reset`: one ring of radius 10; the predator's circle (radius 0.5) at (0, 0); 5 prey circles (radius 0.25), each centre between 5 and 9.75 from (0, 0); 10 pellets, each at least 2 from every prey; everyone acting. On every step: each fish's displacement is its move × 0.1 with the speed capped at 3.0 (predator) or 2.5 (prey), unless stopped at the wall; the predator's centre is never more than 9.5 from (0, 0), a prey's never more than 9.75; there are exactly 10 pellets; a prey is caught exactly when its centre ends the step within 0.75 of the predator's, every such prey in the same step; the predator's reward == the prey caught that step; a caught prey's reward is 0, it is `ended`, never acts again and is absent from every later frame and view; a pellet goes exactly when a prey still swimming after that step's catches ends within 0.5 of it, eaten by the lowest-numbered such prey, which gets +1 per pellet eaten; every other prey reward is 0; each eaten pellet is replaced in the same step by one within 9.5 of (0, 0) and at least 2 from every prey still swimming; no other pellet moves, whatever the predator crosses; prey passing within 0.5 of each other both stay; the game is `done` with `ending == "all_caught"` exactly when the fifth prey goes, else `ending == "time_cap"` at step 600; `score` == (prey caught so far, pellets eaten by all 5 prey so far / 5). Over the 150 games, at least once each: a catch, a step catching two prey, a pellet eaten, an `all_caught` and a `time_cap` ending, the predator's centre within 0.5 of a pellet · **Tolerance:** 1e-9 absolute on positions, distances and scores, float64 geometry; counts, flags, rewards and endings exact; the at-least-once clauses stop the properties passing vacuously

### TC-39: The spec's predator-prey examples play out as stated
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** HUNT-03–HUNT-07, HUNT-09, HUNT-10 · **Kind:** closed form · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-39/
**Source:** the spec's examples: HUNT-03 ("predator choosing velocity (3, 4) … 3.0 units per second in direction (0.6, 0.8) and moves 0.3 units"; "a prey choosing velocity (3, 4) … moves 0.25 units"), HUNT-04 (predator at the wall swimming outward stays touching it), HUNT-05 (prey paths cross; predator over a pellet), HUNT-06 (0.7 caught, 0.8 still swimming), HUNT-07 (0.4 eaten, 0.6 not), HUNT-09, HUNT-10 ("catching 2 prey in one step … +2") and the four HUNT edge cases; a `HuntEnv` reset to each game seed 1000–1019 and driven by a test that reads the frame and picks each move as (target − position) / 0.1, so fish end steps at chosen offsets
**Expected:** (a) from the start: predator move (3, 4) displaces it 0.3 in direction (0.6, 0.8); a prey's move (3, 4) displaces it 0.25 in that direction. (b) the predator swims straight outward until its centre is 9.5 from (0, 0); one more outward move leaves it at 9.5. (c) a prey ending a step 0.7 from the predator is caught: its reward 0, the predator's +1, it has no further steps and no other fish's view shows it; one ending 0.8 away is still swimming. (d) two prey ending the same step within 0.75 are both caught, predator reward +2. (e) a prey ending 0.4 from a pellet eats it: reward +1, a new pellet at least 2 from every prey, still 10 pellets; one ending 0.6 away doesn't. (f) two prey ending within 0.5 of one pellet: the lower-numbered eats it (+1), the other gets 0. (g) a prey ending within 0.5 of a pellet and within 0.75 of the predator is caught and eats nothing; the pellet stays. (h) the predator ending on a pellet's centre leaves the pellet where it was. (i) two prey whose paths cross in a step both finish their moves and both are still swimming. A seed whose setup can't be reached (an earlier catch or the wall in the way) skips that scenario; at least 15 of the 20 seeds must run every scenario · **Tolerance:** 1e-9 absolute, float64 arithmetic on exact decimal inputs; rewards and counts exact; every target distance sits at least 0.05 from its threshold, so rounding can't flip it; the 15-of-20 floor keeps skips from passing the case

### TC-40: Predator and prey see exactly what lies within 4 units
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** HUNT-06, HUNT-12–HUNT-14 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-40/
**Source:** HUNT-12, HUNT-13, HUNT-14 and their examples, the HUNT tie rule, `hunt.PredatorView` and `hunt.PreyView` order, AD-18's relative velocity, and TC-24's three readings of the tank's view (UR-70) carried over; the games of TC-38 (`hunt_games`, seeds 1000–1049, all three lineups), recomputing every view from the frame and the previous frame
**Expected:** the predator's row has 11 values in `PredatorView` order, each acting prey's 14 in `PreyView` order. With radius 4 for everyone, centre to centre: a prey sees the predator exactly when within 4, then `PRED_X, PRED_Y` == predator centre − prey centre and `PRED_VX, PRED_VY` == predator velocity − prey velocity over the step just run ((0, 0) at `reset`); the predator sees the nearest prey still swimming within 4, ties to the lower prey number, with its relative position and velocity the same way; each fish sees the wall exactly when `10 − |p|` ≤ 4, then the offset == `p/|p| · (10 − |p|)`; a prey sees the nearest other prey still swimming within 4, ties to the lower number; each fish sees the nearest pellet within 4, ties to the lower pellet number. Every unseen item's numbers are 0 and its `*_SEEN` 0; every `*_SEEN` is 0.0 or 1.0; a caught prey is never seen. The predator and a prey 3.9–4.0 apart each see the other; 4.0–4.1 apart, neither does. The spec's examples each occur at least once: the predator 2.9–3.1 from a prey (seen), 4.9–5.1 (unseen), the predator with every prey caught before the game ends (`PREY_SEEN` 0 and its 4 numbers 0) · **Tolerance:** 1e-5 absolute, `obs` is float32; flags exact; the at-least-once clauses stop the properties passing vacuously

- **BASE-04** The random swimmer, used for the fish tank's fish, the prey and the predator, picks each move with a uniformly random direction and a speed drawn uniformly from 0 to its fish's top speed: 2.5 units per second for a tank fish or prey, 3.0 for the predator.
  - Given 10,000 random-swimmer moves, when their directions are sorted into four equal quarters of the circle, then each quarter holds between 23% and 27% of them.
  - Given 10,000 random-swimmer moves of a tank fish or prey, when their speeds are averaged, then the mean is between 1.2 and 1.3.
  - Given 10,000 random-predator moves, when their speeds are averaged, then the mean is between 1.45 and 1.55.
- **BASE-05** When the tank's scripted bot sees the ball, it swims at 2.5 units per second directly away from the ball.
  - Given the ball seen at relative position (1, 0), when the bot moves, then its swim velocity is (−2.5, 0).
- **BASE-06** When the tank's scripted bot doesn't see the ball, it stays still.
  - Given the ball seen as no, when the bot moves, then its swim velocity is (0, 0).
- **BASE-07** In each comparison, the baselines play the same setup as the learner they are compared with: in the fish tank, the same fish count and view radius; in predator-prey, the same fixed opponent the learner is tested against (EVAL-01).
  - Given a 5-fish, wide-view learner, when its baselines are scored, then they play the same test tanks with 5 fish and the wide view.
  - Given a learning predator, when the random predator and the scripted chaser are scored, then they play the same test tanks against the scripted forager.
  - Given learning prey, when the random prey and the scripted forager are scored, then they play the same test tanks against the scripted chaser.
- **BASE-08** When the scripted chaser sees a prey, it swims at 3.0 units per second directly toward the nearest prey it sees.
  - Given the nearest prey seen at relative position (1, 0), when the chaser moves, then its swim velocity is (3.0, 0).
- **BASE-09** When the scripted chaser sees no prey, it stays still.
  - Given no prey seen, when the chaser moves, then its swim velocity is (0, 0).
- **BASE-10** When the scripted forager sees the predator, it swims at 2.5 units per second directly away from the predator.
  - Given the predator seen at relative position (0, 2) and a pellet seen, when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (0, −2.5).
- **BASE-11** When the scripted forager doesn't see the predator but sees a pellet, it swims at 2.5 units per second directly toward the nearest pellet it sees.
  - Given the predator seen as no and the nearest pellet at relative position (−3, 0), when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (−2.5, 0).
- **BASE-12** When the scripted forager sees neither the predator nor a pellet, it stays still.
  - Given the predator and every pellet seen as no, when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (0, 0).

**Edge cases & errors**
- No safe move gets closer to the food → the bot takes the safe move that leaves it closest to the food, using the same preference order on ties.
- The tank's scripted bot flees into the wall → it stops at the wall (TANK-05) and keeps swimming away from the ball while it sees it.
- The scripted forager flees into the wall → it stops at the wall (HUNT-04) and keeps swimming away from the predator while it sees it.
- The scripted forager is less than 2.5 units from the nearest pellet → it reaches the pellet on the step it would pass it, at most 0.25 units of swimming.

### TC-42: The random predator spreads its swims evenly up to 3.0
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** BASE-04 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-42/
**Source:** BASE-04's first and third examples; 10,000 `act` calls of `RandomSwimmer(max_speed=3.0)` on a predator start obs (one row each) with `np.random.default_rng(3)`, half with `explore=True`, half `False`
**Expected:** each quarter of the circle (by direction angle, from 0) holds 23%–27% of the moves; the mean speed is 1.45–1.55; every speed is within [0, 3.0]; the draws ignore `explore`; `scores` is `None` · **Tolerance:** the spec's own bands; about 4.6 standard errors wide for a share (√(0.25·0.75/10,000) ≈ 0.0043) and 5.8 for the mean speed (3.0/√12/√10,000 ≈ 0.0087)

### TC-43: Chaser and forager swim as their rules say
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** BASE-08–BASE-12 · **Kind:** closed form · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-43/
**Source:** the spec's examples: BASE-08 (prey seen at (1, 0) → (3.0, 0)), BASE-09 (no prey → (0, 0)), BASE-10 (predator at (0, 2) with a pellet seen → (0, −2.5)), BASE-11 (predator unseen, pellet at (−3, 0) → (−2.5, 0)), BASE-12 (nothing seen → (0, 0)); generator `hunt_view_rows(n=1000, rng_seed=4)`: predator rows and prey rows with each `*_SEEN` 0 or 1, seen positions nonzero, every other number random in [−10, 10]
**Expected:** the five spec rows give exactly their stated velocities; every chaser row with `PREY_SEEN` gives `3.0 · (PREY_X, PREY_Y) / |(PREY_X, PREY_Y)|`, else (0, 0); every forager row with `PRED_SEEN` gives `−2.5 · (PRED_X, PRED_Y) / |·|` whatever the pellet, else with `PELLET_SEEN` `2.5 · (PELLET_X, PELLET_Y) / |·|` (2.5 even when the pellet is nearer than 0.25), else (0, 0); changing any other number changes no move; `scores` is `None` for both · **Tolerance:** 1e-6 absolute, float32 output

### TC-44: The pool keeps the last 10 copies and draws them evenly
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** COEV-04 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-44/
**Source:** COEV-04's two examples and the COEV edge case ("Rung 3 starts before a side has saved a copy → that side's pool holds its starting policy"); a `colearn.Pool` built on a tagged start agent, then 11 tagged copies added one by one; 10,000 `draw`s with `np.random.default_rng(3)` from the full pool
**Expected:** a fresh pool holds the start agent alone and every draw returns it; after the first `add`, the start agent is gone; after the 11th, `copies` holds copies 2–11, oldest first; each of the 10 is drawn 9%–11% of the time, copy 11 (the latest) included; the same rng seed gives the same draws · **Tolerance:** the spec's band, about 3.3 standard errors per share (√(0.1·0.9/10,000) ≈ 0.003); membership and order exact

### TC-45: Each rung's schedule lets the sides learn when its rule says
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** COEV-02–COEV-05 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-45/
**Source:** COEV-02–COEV-05 and their examples, the COEV edge case on an empty pool, AD-17; `registry.make_schedule("hunt", {"match": m}, …)` for each match `rung-1`, `rung-2`, `rung-3`, `control-predator`, `control-prey` with `colearn.swap_every` 20, `colearn.copy_every` 20, `colearn.pool_size` 10, over test-only counting learners (each `observe` adds one update; `frozen()` returns a tagged copy) driven through 300 games of 30 steps, `start_game` with `np.random.default_rng(5)`, `current` after every step
**Expected:** `rung-1`: every lineup has both sides learning, each played by its learner; `versions` 10. `rung-2`: the predator learns first, the prey played by a frozen copy; straight after the predator's 20th update of its turn, `current` shows no side learning; the prey starts learning at the next `start_game`, against a frozen predator; turns keep alternating every 20 updates of the learning side, and a side's updates never grow outside its turn; `versions` 10. `rung-3`: turns as `rung-2`; at each `start_game` the side not learning is a copy drawn from its pool with the given rng, never its learner, and never learns; before a side's first copy its pool holds its starting policy; each side adds a copy at every 20th update of its own; `versions` 10. `control-predator`: the predator always learns, every prey move comes from `ForageBot`; `control-prey`: the prey always learn, every predator move from `ChaseBot`; `versions` 0. The same rng seed gives the same lineups; `rung-4` raises `UnknownName` · **Tolerance:** exact, counts and lineups are discrete

**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** RUN-01–RUN-04, EVAL-01, EVAL-02, BASE-07, PG-06, COEV-01, COEV-03, COEV-05, TOUR-01, DATA-01 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-46/
**Source:** RUN-01's predator-prey example, RUN-04's, EVAL-01's and EVAL-02's predator-prey clauses, BASE-07's two predator-prey examples, PG-06's prey example, COEV-03's and COEV-05's examples, TOUR-01's example, AD-05, AD-11, AD-17; `train_run` twice for `pg-ppo` `rung-2`, seed 1, 4,000 steps, 20 curve points, `colearn.swap_every` 2 (so turns swap within the run), inside an env wrapper that records every `reset` seed and agent wrappers that record every `act` and `observe`; once for `pg-ppo` `control-prey`, seed 1, 2,000 steps, 4 curve points; plus `random` and `scripted` records on side `prey` and side `predator`

### TC-48: Predator-prey comparisons pair each side with its own baselines and rungs, and stay in their own report

### TC-49: A planted trend is found; a cycle, a one-sided rise and noise are not
**Pipeline:** PL-02 · **Covers:** TOUR-04, EVAL-06 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-49/
**Source:** TOUR-04 and its two examples, AD-19 (both trend tests must be significant, `p_adjusted` the larger); generator `fake_grids`, 5 seed runs' 10 × 10 grids per rung, each cell clipped to [0, 5] and rounded to a multiple of 0.05 (a mean of 20 catch counts): (a) seed 19, cell(i, j) = 2.5 + 0.2(i − 5.5) − 0.2(j − 5.5) + Normal(0, 0.3); (b) seed 20, cycle: cell(i, j) = 2.5 + 1.5 sin(2π(i − j)/10) + Normal(0, 0.3); (c) seed 21, one-sided: cell(i, j) = 2.5 + 0.2(i − 5.5) + Normal(0, 0.3); (d) seeds 22–41, every cell Uniform(0, 5). Given to `null-hypothesis-testing` with each seed run's 100 pairing scores: H0 no rise of catches with predator version (and, separately, no fall with prey version), within the report's correction over 28 tests
**Expected:** (a) `trend`, both tests rejecting H0, `p_adjusted` the larger of the two; (b) `no_trend`, marked unexplained, and explained once `reports/hunt/explanations/<id>.md` holds text; (c) `no_trend`, the prey-version test not rejecting; (d) `no_trend` · **Tolerance:** (a) must hold, a 1.8-catch rise across versions is many standard errors of 0.3 noise; (b), (c) must hold; (d) at most 1 of the 20 seeds may give `trend`, the family-wise error rate is α = 0.05
**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing

**Pipeline:** PL-03 · **Covers:** REPLAY-01, REPLAY-06, REPLAY-09, REPLAY-10, RUN-09 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-52/
**Source:** REPLAY-06, REPLAY-09's scripted-side example, REPLAY-10 and its example, AD-07, AD-18; runs from `train_run`: `pg-ppo` `rung-2`, seed 1, 4,000 steps, 20 curve points; `pg-ppo` `control-prey`, seed 1, 2,000 steps; the `scripted` record on side `predator`
**Expected:** for every saved game of every tested side, `open_game(index=i, side=s)` gives no `ReplayMismatch`, and `moves`, `scores`, `score_after` and `ending` equal to the saved game, with one more frame than moves; with no `side`, the rung-2 run opens its predator games, and `side="prey"` with `index=5` opens its sixth prey game; every frame holds the ring, the predator, each prey still swimming and 10 pellets, caught prey absent; its labels hold the time in seconds (0.1 × step), the pellets eaten so far, the prey caught so far and one capture time per prey caught so far; the last frame's ending is `all_caught` or `time_cap`; each learning side's `scores` give one spread per acting agent per step, a scripted side's are `None`; tampering one saved `score_after` raises `ReplayMismatch` at that step · **Tolerance:** exact, replays rebuild the same floats

### TC-53: A tournament plays all 100 version pairings on test seeds 0–19
**Pipeline:** PL-04 · **Covers:** TOUR-02 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-53/
**Source:** TOUR-02 and its example, HUNT-11, AD-19; `play_tournament` on fake complete `rung-1` runs whose saved versions are stand-ins: (a) every predator and prey version a test-only `still` agent that always swims (0, 0); (b) predator versions alternating `ChaseBot` (odd) and `RandomSwimmer(3.0)` (even), prey versions alternating `ForageBot` (odd) and `RandomSwimmer(2.5)` (even), loaded through recording wrappers; (c) a complete `control-predator` run; (d) a `rung-2` run missing `versions/prey-7.pt`
**Expected:** (a) every cell is 0: the predator starts at the centre and every prey at least 5 away, and nobody moves. (b) a 10 × 10 grid, row `i` predator version `i`, column `j` prey version `j`; each pairing is played on seeds 0–19 in order, both sides with `explore=False`, each version loaded by `load_agent(version=k)`; each cell equals the mean predator score of `play_game` on that pairing over seeds 0–19, a multiple of 0.05 in [0, 5]; `seeds` is (0, …, 19), none a training seed; the same record twice gives the same grid. (c) `ValueError`, not a ladder run. (d) `FileNotFoundError` naming `prey-7` · **Tolerance:** exact, catches are counts and the mean of 20 counts is exact in float64 to 1e-12

full text
I've added golden cases TC-38 to TC-56 for the predator–prey stage to `docs/packages/rllab/tests.md`, with fixtures under `tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-38/` to `TC-56/`. The earlier cases TC-01 to TC-37 are unchanged. The doc is back to **Draft** until you approve the expected outputs below.

`dt status` now shows no gaps: every pipeline has a smoke test and at least one golden case. `dt lint` has 0 errors and 1 warning: the file is 320 lines, over its 300-line limit for one package (open question 7).

## 1. What was written

**Strategy table changes**

| Pipeline | Smoke test added | Golden cases added | Model eval |
|---|---|---|---|
| PL-01 | `rllab train --env hunt --learner pg-ppo --match rung-3 --seed 1 --smoke` | TC-38 to TC-41 (the environment), TC-42 and TC-43 (baselines), TC-44 (the pool), TC-45 (the schedules), TC-46 (a whole run), TC-56 (whole run, regression) | + TC-47 |
| PL-02 | — | TC-48 to TC-51 | — |
| PL-03 | — | TC-52 | — |
| PL-04 (new row) | `rllab tournament --smoke` | TC-53, TC-54 | — |
| PL-05 (new row) | `rllab replay --env hunt --predator scripted --prey scripted --seed 1 --smoke` | TC-55 | — |

**The cases.** There are no real input/output pairs from you this round. Every case is a generator plus a property, or a spec example checked word for word.

| ID | Kind | Source | Expected | Tolerance and why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-38 | synthetic | HUNT rules; seeds 1000–1049; three lineups (random vs random, chaser vs forager, chaser vs a test-only "grazer" that only goes for pellets) | Every step obeys the spec's limits: speed caps 3.0 and 2.5, the predator's centre at most 9.5 from the middle and a prey's at most 9.75, always 10 pellets. A catch happens exactly within 0.75, eating exactly within 0.5. Pellet ties go to the lower-numbered prey, and a prey caught that step eats nothing. Respawns are at least 2 from every prey. Score is (catches, pellets ÷ 5). Endings are `all_caught` / `time_cap` at step 600. Each of these happens at least once: a catch, a double catch, a pellet eaten, both endings, the predator over a pellet | 1e-9, float64 geometry; counts exact |
| TC-39 | closed form | spec examples, with the test steering fish from the frame; seeds 1000–1019 | Spec examples vs expected: predator (3,4) → moves 0.3 along (0.6,0.8); prey (3,4) → 0.25. At the wall the predator stays at 9.5. A prey 0.7 from the predator is caught (+1 to the predator, 0 to the prey), 0.8 is not. Two catches in one step → +2. A prey 0.4 from a pellet eats it (+1), 0.6 does not. On a shared pellet the lower-numbered prey eats. Caught beside a pellet → eats nothing. Predator over a pellet → no effect. Prey crossing paths → both survive | 1e-9; targets at least 0.05 from each threshold; at least 15 of 20 seeds must run every scenario |
| TC-40 | synthetic | HUNT-12–14, on TC-38's games | 11 / 14 view numbers in contract order. Everything is seen within radius 4, centre to centre. 3.9 apart: both see each other; 4.1 apart: neither. Unseen items are all zeros. Caught prey are never seen | 1e-5, the views are float32 |
| TC-41 | synthetic | HUNT-15 / HUNT-02; seeds 1000–1019 | Same seed and moves give an identical game, respawns included; seeds 1000 and 1001 start differently | exact |
| TC-42 | synthetic | BASE-04; 10,000 random-predator moves | Each quarter of the circle gets 23–27%; mean speed 1.45–1.55; speeds stay in [0, 3.0] | the spec's bands (4.6 and 5.8 standard errors) |
| TC-43 | closed form | BASE-08–12 examples + 1,000 generated view rows | Spec examples vs expected: (1,0) → (3.0, 0); no prey → (0, 0); predator at (0,2) → (0, −2.5); pellet at (−3,0) → (−2.5, 0); nothing seen → (0, 0). Generated rows follow the same rules | 1e-6, float32 |
| TC-44 | synthetic | COEV-04 + its edge case | A fresh pool holds the starting policy. After 11 copies it keeps copies 2–11. Each is drawn 9–11% of 10,000 times | the spec's band, about 3.3 standard errors |
| TC-45 | synthetic | COEV-02–05, using test-only counting learners | rung-1: both sides learn. rung-2: the predator learns first, swapping every 20 updates. rung-3: turns, plus pool draws that never learn. Controls: the opponent is always the scripted bot, versions 0. `rung-4` raises `UnknownName` | exact |
| TC-46 | synthetic | `train_run`: rung-2 (20 curve points, swap every 2), control-prey, and the 4 baseline records | Folder layout. Training uses seeds ≥1000 only. The predator is tested against the forager and the prey against the chaser, on seeds 0–99. Each curve point has both sides' scores. 10 versions per side, saved at points 2, 4, …, 20, and version 10 matches the final agent. The run reruns identically. The control-prey run saves only `agent-prey` | exact; scores 1e-9 relative |
| TC-47 | model eval | 5 real rung-3 runs + tournament | Predator beats the random predator and beats or ties the chaser. Prey beat the random prey and beat or tie the forager. Rung-3 tournament shows a trend, or no trend with a written explanation. Each run under 60 minutes | α 0.05 corrected over 28 tests (`null-hypothesis-testing`) |
| TC-48 | synthetic | fake runs (seed 18), with rung-3's `predator.lr` changed | 16 baseline comparisons, each side against its own baselines. The control steps carry their note. rung-2 → rung-3 waits, naming `predator.lr`. Correction is over 28 tests. 8 curve charts. The Snake and tank reports are unchanged | exact |
| TC-49 | synthetic | planted / cycle / one-sided / noise grids (seeds 19–41) | planted → trend; cycle → no trend, unexplained until written; one-sided rise → no trend; noise → no trend | at most 1 of the 20 noise seeds may show a trend (α = 0.05) |
| TC-50 | synthetic | rung-1 with 5 grids; rung-2 missing seed 3's grid; rung-3 with seed 5 still running | Only rung-1 gets a chart, each cell the mean of its 5 grids. Waiting names seed 3 and seed 5 | 1e-12 |
| TC-51 | synthetic | recorded comparisons (a)–(e) | (a) done; (b) not done: rung-3 no-trend unexplained; (c) not done: rung-3 prey lose to the forager; (d) not done: rung-1's tournament waiting; (e) done, because a rung-2 loss doesn't block it | exact |
| TC-52 | synthetic | rung-2, control-prey and scripted-predator runs | Every saved game of either side replays with no mismatch. Frames show the time, pellets eaten, prey caught and capture times. A scripted side's scores are None. A tampered game raises `ReplayMismatch` at that step | exact |
| TC-53 | synthetic | stand-in versions in a fake rung-1 run | (a) every agent stands still → every cell 0. (b) A 10×10 grid, each cell the mean of 20 games on seeds 0–19, played greedily. (c) a control run → `ValueError`. (d) missing prey-7 → `FileNotFoundError` | exact |
| TC-54 | synthetic | runs folder with one grid already made, a broken run, a control, Snake and tank runs | Plays only the missing grids, reports the failing run without raising, and leaves existing grids and run folders byte for byte. A second call plays nothing new | exact |
| TC-55 | synthetic | the TC-46 runs, seed 12345 | Saved predator vs saved prey plays the same game twice. A `scripted` side is the forager / chaser, its scores shown "n/a". Error cases raise as the contract says | exact |
| TC-56 | regression | Snake + tank snapshot taken when the fish-tank stage is accepted | With the predator–prey code present, the snapshot runs rerun, compare and replay identically | exact, apart from wall-clock times |

## 2. ASSUMPTIONS
- Seeds 1000–1049 for environment games and 3/4/5/9 for random generators, following the fish-tank cases; 18–41 for the fake runs and grids; 12345 for the fresh pairing.
- TC-46 sets swap every 2 so turns swap inside a short test run.
- TC-49's grid formulas: slope 0.2 per version, cycle amplitude 1.5, noise σ 0.3.
- TC-38's "at least once" requirements may fail if 150 games never produce them, for example a double catch. If that happens the case comes back to you; I won't loosen it.

## 3. Open questions (also recorded in tests.md)
1. Views: TC-40 reuses your three readings for the fish-tank views (UR-70): distances centre to centre, relative velocity over the last step, nearest wall point on radius 10.
2. Settings belong to the whole run, so rung-2 → rung-3 waits for both sides when `predator.lr` differs (TC-48).
3. A chart is only an image path, so TC-50 checks the cell means on the values passed to the plot, not by reading the PNG.
4. TC-47 is a manual acceptance check (5 runs of up to an hour each, plus a tournament), like TC-21 and TC-32.
5. TC-56's snapshot is made and its hashes written only when the fish-tank stage is accepted, each write approved by you (as for TC-37).
6. No test hook is needed: TC-39 steers fish by choosing their moves, and TC-53 uses saved stand-in agents.
7. **tests.md is now 320 lines**, over the 300-line limit. Should `architecture-design` consider splitting `rllab`? I haven't sent it there; that's your call.
- Also: I updated the old TC-29 open question to say its test hooks were added under AD-15. `dt lint` flagged it because the routed change had already landed.

## 4. Uncovered
- **Pipelines:** none.
- **Spec IDs:**
  - QR-02 (runs on the laptop) is checked by hand.
  - PG-08's "written in the project" has no test for the predator side.
  - RUN-04's two-score display is checked only through the `CurvePoint` keys, not the CLI's progress line.

## 5. Approval
Do you approve the expected outputs of TC-38 to TC-56, and the answer to question 7: should `architecture-design` consider splitting `rllab`?
